# Moonstone Rebuilt – fixes and improvements

Addresses are in the unpacked programs: `1000:xxxx` is code in `MAIN.EXE`, `DS:xxxx` its data, `INTR` the intro
program. Every patch checks the original bytes first, so it is never applied to a different file.
*Tested* means confirmed in play; *from the code* means worked out from the program and checked in an emulator, but
not yet confirmed in play.

## Bug fixes in the original game

| ID | Fix | Status |
|---|---|---|
| K1 | **A sound that never played.** The sample name table lists `cheap2b` twice (DS:8721, entries 8 and 9); the list is alphabetical, so entry 8 is meant to be `cheap1b`, and CHEAP1B never played. | from the code |
| K2 | **Ratmen and the moon.** The manual says ratmen's strength rises as the moon fills, but the game made them strong at full moon and strongest at new moon (1000:23FE). Now: strongest at full moon (12 hit points, bite 8, claw 5), strong at ¾ moon (7 / 6 / 3), normal otherwise. | from the code |
| K4 | **Object slots can run out in fights.** Fight objects such as fire, hit effects and daggers need a game slot (20) and an animation slot (only 10). When the animation slots were full, the game slot stayed taken for the rest of the fight (1000:3E89); with all 20 gone, the next object was written past the table (DS:76B0) over the fight's own data. The dragon fight makes the most objects when it is fought from a distance (fire breath, daggers). Now a slot that gets no animation slot is freed at once (1000:3EA5, 10 bytes). In play the limit is hard to reach (a dragon fight with fire and three daggers in the air uses about 8–9 of the 10 animation slots), so this is probably **not** the cause of the dragon fight hang reported on GOG; that cause is still open. | tested: no side effects |
| K5 | **Game Over on the Q key.** On the map, pressing Q ended the game at once, without asking, during any knight's turn (1000:A594). Q is next to W and A, the second player's movement keys, so the game could end by accident: the likely cause of the "unexpected Game Over" reports on GOG. Game Over is now on Esc (one byte). | tested |
| K11 | **Math and Mythral read their stat tables wrongly.** Math's stat gift (1000:B9C2) stepped through its table at the wrong stride: endurance never rose, half of the gifts went to a value that is not a stat, and Math's line was read from a wrong address (in play: a black screen and a long list of the game's internal data). Mythral (1000:BBD5) showed the wrong message, or none, for the stat it changed. Now Math gives strength, endurance or constitution (5/16, 5/16, 6/16) with the matching line, and Mythral the right message. Losing to the Guardian now always costs a real stat point, as intended. | tested |
| K16 | **Duel looting.** When a knight took the loser's weapon and that weapon was the Sword of Sharpness, only the weapon moved, not the sword itself: the loser kept the sword and was armed with it again, so both knights had one (1000:CE91). The sword now moves with the weapon (the code is in `SAVEHOOK.COM`, which needs to be running, as for D6). Also: when a computer knight won and took better armour, the hit points that moved with it came from its own old armour (1000:0B8E); a computer knight's stat raise after a duel win had no limit of 5, unlike a player's (1000:04B0); and when the loser had no lives left, the sword was set as the dead loser's weapon (1000:0C4B, harmless). | from the code |
| K17 | **Ratmen disease removed.** A ratman's bite infected the knight, and every four days (each moon phase) a diseased knight lost a life point until healed, without any message in the game, so players took it for a bug. The bite no longer infects, and a knight infected in an older save no longer loses lives to it. | from the code |
| K18 | **The dragon's hoard grows with its victories, not from game to game.** Every new game (and every practice) added four magic items and gold to the hoard without clearing it, so after several games in one session it held far more than one game's worth. Now it is rolled anew at each new game, and every time the dragon beats a knight it gets one more magic item at a 50 % chance and 0-9 gold (1000:1DBE, 0D23; needs `SAVEHOOK.COM`). `custom_rules.ini`: `dragon_hoard = reset` (no growth) or `keep` (the original, for speedruns). | emulator-tested; not yet tried in play |
| K19 | **Moonstone names.** The item screen called one of the two half-moon Moonstones "New moon Moonstone". The names now follow Stonehenge's check, which decides the win (1000:1053), and the icons: one new-moon, one full-moon and two half-moon stones (1000:C766). | emulator-tested; not yet tried in play |
| K20 | **Moon pictures in the wrong order.** The map showed full, half, three-quarters, crescent, new: the half and three-quarter pictures were swapped (the cycle DS:05A9). They are drawn the other way round now (1000:9053), so the half moon shows on the days the half-moon stones count. The days themselves do not change. | emulator-tested; not yet tried in play |
| K25 | **Instant kills in co-op hit the wrong knight.** The monsters' instant kills (troll, mudman, Balok, dragon...) set the HP of the one-to-one fight's knight to -1 (`1000:0AB2`); in co-op that was the first knight standing, while the knight hit played its death, stood up again and stayed the monster's target. Now the knight the monster fights dies. The knight's handler (`1000:3ED0`) never looked at its HP, so in co-op a knight down now gets the death fall once and stays; the troll looked only at knight 1's HP (`1000:55F9`) and now looks at the knight it fights. A knight down goes off the screen after its fall; a mudman that left with a knight still counted as on the screen, so no other came; now the game's own top-up (`1000:0243`) brings the next. | not yet tried in play A knight down leaves no body behind, and a mudman pulls under (or lets go of) the knight it holds, not another (`1000:9AFB`, `52FE`, `559C`, `54DB`). |
| K26 | **The troll swung at the air.** The troll counted as level with its knight within 5 in depth either way, but its swing hits only when its depth is 0-4 more than the knight's. 1-5 nearer, it stood swinging at the air for as long as the knight stood still - for example a knight at the screen's edge with the troll off the screen. Now it steps until it is level enough to hit (`1000:570E`). It also swung from 91 away on, where its stick passes a standing knight (that needs about 100); now it steps back within 104 (`1000:573B`). |
| K28 | **The Balok froze a knight in co-op.** The Balok's hold pauses the knight it touched (`1000:37A7`) but let go of the knight it fights at that moment (`37FC`, read from `[0978h]`), which with 2-4 knights could be another one: the knight held stayed frozen and the fight stuck. The knight it takes hold of now stays its knight while the hold lasts (`35AF`), and the Balok's frames of the hold show that knight in its own colours (they were always blue). The Balok's bite and squeeze, and the mudman's pull from below, also kill from their scripts, which took the knight nearest the monster: they now kill the knight it holds. The spear trogg's frames of a knight on its spear (`2F90`) show that knight in its own colours. |
| K29 | **The dragon's blind spot.** The dragon always faces right (`1000:3C2B` sets it after `3CFF` has turned it to its knight) and keeps to x 30-100, so a knight at the left edge, behind it, was never attacked while its own blows hurt the dragon. At the dragon's turn (the update hook, `98FD`) the knights of the fight are kept at least 20 (the distance the dragon backs off from, +54h) right of its x, the object and its animation. | from the code |
| K30 | **Monsters' special attacks in the knight's colours.** In co-op the frames of the ratmen (RATMEN2.CEL 42-62), the boar (BE1.C 34-61), the mudmen (MUDMEN1/2.CEL) and the Guardian (DEMON2/3.CEL) that show a knight were drawn in the first knight's colours; they now take the colours of the knight the monster goes for (D15), at the draw hook (`9AFD`, `9B09`). | from the code |
| K31 | **A dagger thrown to the left never left the fight.** The dagger's handler (`1000:3E6F`) ended it only with its x between -10 and 0, and it flies 20 a frame, so most daggers thrown to the left skipped that window and flew on for good. They filled the fight's 20 object slots, and then no dagger could be thrown at all (the Black Knights' throws showed nothing). It now ends at x -10 or less (`3E7D`). | from the code |
| K32 | **The computer knights kept to a few lairs.** Their lair choice sorts the 24 lairs by distance (`1000:A986`), but each swap stored a value in the wrong field (`A9AA`), so only about 4 different lairs were left in the table. Speedrun mode keeps the game's own behaviour. | emulated |
| K33 | **Limits and checks.** The game ends when no human knight can play again (a dead knight and a frog gave a false Game Over; two dead knights with a computer knight between them never ended; `A67B`). HP stay at 1 or more when armour is taken (`CE75`, `0BAD`), and automatic loot moves the HP of the armour actually taken (`0BAD`). A flight with no free path slot lands where it is (`2CFB`). Mythral's table is not read past its end (`BC2E`). Limits are tested with >= instead of = (daggers `CE1A`, `CE26`; gold `CEF8`), and a knight below 0 lives is not fought by the dragon (`0D13`). | emulated |
| K34 | **A lair's monster count overwritten.** A lair fight leaves `[898Dh]` = 2 until the next turn (`B1B0`), and every fight's end (`039E`) stored its own count in the last lair while it was 2, so a dragon attack in the same turn changed that lair. The end of a fight now clears the mark. | tested in Unicorn, not played |
| K27 | **The dragon breathed fire at the air.** The dragon keeps to the left of the screen and took up its far fire only for a knight 140 or more away; its near fire does not reach 96, so a knight 96-139 away was never hurt. The far fire now starts at 96 (`1000:388C`). |
| K24 | **Moonstone damage bonus at the wrong phase.** In its phase a Moonstone doubles the knight's damage, but the new-moon stone did so at half moon and one half-moon stone at new moon, the other way round from Stonehenge and the stones' names and icons (1000:2DC1, 2DE2). | emulator-tested; not yet tried in play |
| K6 | **The dragon and the Guardian could be stun-locked.** Every hit restarted their hit reaction, so quick blows (the upward thrust under the dragon) kept them from ever fighting back. After a hit that counts, further hits do not count for a short time (`dragon_hit_recovery`, `guardian_hit_recovery`; 0.8 s; 1000:3861, 4F6A). | emulator-tested; the time needs a play test |
| K21 | **The dragon's target could be rerolled.** Every time the map was drawn anew (for example after the item screen), the dragon picked a new random target (1000:A78C). It now keeps a target that still has lives; the Scroll of the Wyrm still picks a new one. | emulator-tested; not yet tried in play |
| K22 | **The extra-turn glitch.** After a fight or a visit that used up the turn, the game read the keys again before ending the turn, so Enter pressed at the right moment started another fight or visit the same day (1000:A5B8). | emulator-tested; not yet tried in play |
| K23 | **Lives back after losing to the Guardian.** The Guardian takes three lives and could take them below zero; a healing potion then gave the knight five lives (an unsigned compare, 1000:CCB6). A knight without lives now gets none from a potion. | emulator-tested; not yet tried in play |
| K10 | **Sound effects at the wrong speed.** The DOS sound files are the Amiga version's samples byte for byte, but every DOS file says 16,129 Hz, while the Amiga plays them at 4,766–22,655 Hz. Most sounds played too fast and too high (the dragon's grunts 3.4 times). Each file now carries its real rate (one byte per file; the rates come from the Amiga version, no Amiga files needed). | tested |
| K35 | **Many sounds played the wrong sample.** The DOS code and animation scripts ask for the Amiga version's sound numbers (every sound routine has the Amiga's constants), but the Sound Blaster table (`DS:7E6E`, sound id → sample, 364 entries) was filled in against another numbering, so about 50 of the ids the game uses played another sample although the right one was loaded. Among them: ids 82-109 were one entry off (one rat leap in four played the crush sound, half the mud voices played `STRETCH`, the mudmen screamed like rats); the Guardian's screams played the dragon's muffled cry or the beast's roar; the beast's own roar and the troll's grunts played the default `HIT3`; a knight in the dragon's mouth sounded like a troll; the hit after a Balok's thrash was a sword clash; the troggs' blood, spear and slice sounds were rotated. Three routines asked for the wrong ids: the mudmen's scare voice (`1000:57D8`) for a grunt or the head chop, the troll's grunt (`1000:5801`) for the Balok's roars, and the rat on the skull (`1000:3DC1`) for the impale sound half the time. All now play what the Amiga plays, or the closest DOS sample where DOS has none (rat bite, strain, the beast's gallop). Ids nothing asks for are unchanged. Moonstone Rebuilt's own sounds have ids from 171 up (the mines used id 26). No Amiga files needed. | tested in Unicorn, not listened to |
| K36 | **Every sound at one pitch.** The Amiga plays the same sample at a different rate for each sound (footsteps at three pitches, the Balok's roars at four, the troll's grunts 2.5 times slower than the file's rate, the beast's roar 1.7 times faster); DOS played one rate per file, and K10's rates are the files' own, not the game's. The sound routine now notes the id (`1000:5995`), and before the driver call (`1000:9537`, int 63h) SAVEHOOK.COM writes that id's time constant into the copied sample (table at `DS:7F22`; ids whose DOS sample is a stand-in keep the file's rate). Rates above 22,727 Hz are capped. | tested in Unicorn, not listened to |
| K37 | **Whole samples where the Amiga plays a part.** The dragon's four grunts are one file and its three chomps another; the Amiga plays one grunt or one chomp per sound (ids 20-26, 49-51), DOS played the whole file each time: three chomps for every bite. The K36 handler now plays only the id's part (list at `DS:7DC2`): it writes a sample block header before the part and an end mark after it in the copy of the sample made for that sound. | tested in Unicorn, not listened to |
| K38 | **A blocked blow was silent.** The block check (`1000:4219`) is the Amiga's routine with the call that plays the sword clash (sound 17) left out: it loaded the number and returned. In the original the clash was heard when a knight was struck instead, because the hit's id played the clash sample (K35). The end of the routine is rearranged in its own 14 bytes so that a block that holds plays the clash. The knight's grunt when struck (`1000:3D66`, also switched off in the DOS code) stays off: with one voice it would cut the hit. | tested in Unicorn, not listened to |
| K39 | **A faint high whine whenever the game was quiet.** When MAIN.EXE starts it programs timer channel 2 to 23.9 kHz and switches the PC speaker on (`1000:0049`-`005F`: mode B6h, divisor 32h, `or` 3 into port 61h), and nothing switches it off. On a real PC nobody hears that tone. DOSBox-X's mixer (48 kHz) turns it into tones at about 5.6 and 6.3 kHz, quiet (RMS 0.003 of full scale) but plain when nothing else plays: in the first menu while the Amiga intro music is on (the menu's own tune is then a muted copy), where a nine-year-old heard it at once. The game makes no other use of the speaker, so `moonstone-dosbox-x.conf` has `[speaker] pcspeaker = false`; the programs are not changed, and the setting also covers original mode (Fixes_Off.bat). | measured by captures from DOSBox-X: the tones are there with every other mixer channel muted and gone with the speaker's channel muted; with the setting the mixer has no speaker channel and the menu is silent (RMS 0.0001). Not yet confirmed by ear |
| K40 | **The Guardian hid knights for good in co-op.** The Guardian takes a knight off the screen with the game's pause toggle (`1000:9892`, the animation's +22h): its whip catches a knight (`5155`, `518A`) and lets it go (`50E0`, `511B`), and its zap's script hides the knight with a 10 HP blow (KnightOFF, `5254`) and shows it beside the Guardian again (KnightON, `5262`). Each read the knight from `[0978h]`. In co-op that is set for each object in turn (D15), and the zap's script runs in the drawing pass, where it is whichever object came last: the zap hid one knight and toggled another, so both stayed hidden while the fight went on (the user's save: one knight at 0 HP hidden, the other hidden too). A blow on the Guardian during the hold (DemonStruck, `5202`) or its own hit (`5245`) also ended the hold or the zap without showing the knight, which a second knight (or a dagger in flight) can do. Now the knight hidden is noted (`demon_held`): the zap takes the knight the Guardian goes for (D15's `last_target`; one-to-one: `[0978h]` as before), the game's own let-go and KnightON show that knight and place it, a hold ended early shows it at once, and the Guardian's frames of the hold draw it in its own colours (K30). Fixed MAIN.EXE with SAVEHOOK.COM. | tested in Unicorn (`tests/test_guardian.py`), not played |
| K41 | **The Balok hid a knight for good.** The Balok's hold toggles the pause of the knight it touched (`1000:37A7`) and toggles the knight in `[0978h]` again at the let-go (`3810`). Two Baloks that touch the knight in the same frame both grabbed it (the second grab showed it again, so the let-go hid it for good), and a grab of anything else it touched (a dagger) paused that while the let-go toggled the knight: the knight vanished and the fight went on (the user's save: a lair fight one to one, the knight at full HP, hidden). Now a grab takes only a knight that has an animation not already paused; anything else gets the Balok's plain hit (`379E`). The let-go acts on the knight held (K28's `balok_victim`, now in every fight, not only co-op) and only if it is hidden; else it is neither toggled nor placed beside the Balok (`3840`). Fixed MAIN.EXE with SAVEHOOK.COM. | tested in Unicorn (`tests/test_guardian.py`, `test_bk.py`), not played |
| K42 | **Co-op colours of a knight's death.** The Guardian's zap that kills a knight (KnightON, `1000:52BC`) blacks out the fight palette's entries 2-7 for good, to show the knight burnt. In a co-op lair those entries are every knight's greys, so all knights went black when one died. In co-op the palette now stays (the handler `demon_char` skips to `52E8`), the knight killed is noted (`charred`) and only its frames are drawn burnt: the Black Knight's colours for pixels 6-8 and, in the recolour pass, its pixels 1-5 as the darkest of them (`char_piece`). One to one the game's blackout is kept. The boar's toss and impaling frames (BE1.C, played on the boar by BeastStruck1 `4453` / `4487`) show the knight in the colours of the knight the boar goes for (K30, `last_target`), which changes to another knight once the one struck is down: the blue knight's death was drawn green. `mov si,[di+0Eh]` there now notes the knight struck and the boar (`boar_victim`, `boar_obj`), and the draw uses that knight for the boar's frames. The other monsters' frames of a knight already use the knight held (Balok K28/K41, dragon K25, mudman K25, ratman, spear trogg, Guardian K40). Patch `K42` in `patches.py` (`52BC`, `4453`, `4487`), handlers `rules_demon_char`, `rules_boar_struck`; the fight's end clears the three words. Test: `tests/test_guardian.py`. |
| K43 | **The troll swung at the air** (user report 14 Nov 2026, slot 3). The troll's step (56E5h) counts its knight as in reach at a distance of at most `[si+52h]` (150; 3CBFh, bp from 1000:5745), but its attack picks the chop only from 100 to 149 (`cmp ax,96h` at 5690h): at exactly 150 it took the swing, which reaches about 100, and swung at the air for as long as the knight stood still (the troll walks 5 at a time, so it often stops at exactly 150). `mov bp,[si+52h]` at 1000:5745 becomes int 63h (`troll_reach`): for the troll the reach is 149, so it steps in to 145 and chops. Also `troll_target` (55F9h, K25): outside co-op the troll tested knight 1's HP, not that of the knight it fights; with knight 1 dead it stood idle. Now [0978h] whenever it is one of the four knights' records. Run Setup.bat again. |

## Improvements

| ID | Improvement | Status |
|---|---|---|
| K9 | **Skip the intro and the ending**: the intro with any key but Ctrl, Alt, Shift and the lock keys, the ending with Enter, Space or Esc. The intro reads the keyboard through its own interrupt handler (`4774`); its store into the key table (`477F`) is a far call to K9's cave, which notes a fresh press of a key that skips (a make code of a key that was released, so not a key's repeat; the mode word `[12D1h]` tells the intro from the ending). The press is acted on, once the music module is loaded, at three places: the retrace wait (`0F9E`), the start of every frame of the scenes' frame loops (`021F`, the far call at `0228`) and every pass of the first sequence (`0D76`, D87): the stack is reset and the intro's own shutdown (`00D1`) runs, which stops the music. Until 15 Nov 2026 the skip looked only in the retrace wait, at keys held then; the frame loops (`0162`, `0694`) and the first sequence do not wait for retraces, so a short press of Enter or Space there was missed (the game's own Esc check at `00EA`, in every frame loop, still works). | tested; captured in DOSBox-X (15 Nov 2026) |
| D1 | **DOSBox-X with save states:** 100 slots, F11+S and F11+L. | tested |
| D2 | **Better music:** Roland MT-32, General MIDI (SoundFont) or the original AdLib music. The General MIDI modules are the game's Roland modules with the instruments remapped. | MT-32 and GM need your own ROMs / SoundFont |
| D4 | **Settings confirmed by the GOG community:** speed 4,000 cycles (the GOG default 9,000 is about twice too fast), joystick drift fix (`timed = false`), OpenGL output against the black screen. | speed tested |
| D5 | **The intro works in DOSBox-X.** The packed original programs (EXEPACK) fail when DOSBox-X loads them into the lowest 64 KB, so the intro was skipped and the original MAIN.EXE stayed black. Rebuilt uses unpacked programs in both modes; in original mode (`Fixes_Off.bat`) they are only unpacked, otherwise unchanged. (An earlier version started the packed originals with `LOADFIX`, which left too little memory: *Insufficient memory*.) | tested |
| D6 | **Automatic save before every fight** (slots 96–100). DOSBox-X cannot be told to save from inside DOS, but it can save on a timer into separate slots per running program. When a fight starts (1000:0307), the helper `SAVEHOOK.COM` renames the game to `FIGHT1`-`FIGHT5` in turn for about a second, one slot per name: a second save in the same wait (DOSBox-X's save pauses it) goes into the same slot instead of the next. | tested; one slot per name not yet tested |
| D7 | **Automatic save at the start of every human turn** on the map (slots 91–94, `TURN1`-`TURN4`; 1000:A63F). Computer knights' turns are not saved. The wait ends as soon as the save's file (`C:\SAVE\91.SAV` ...) has a new date and time, at most about 1.1 s as before (0.7.0). Every turn then plays the game's menu sound (sound 0Fh, as `1000:D6E1`), the computer's turns too (0.7.0). | tested |
| D9 | **Player 2 moves with W, A, S, D.** The original used W (up), A (left), D (right) and X (down); down is now S (1000:8403). Fire stays on Tab. | tested |
| D11 | **Practice against the computer.** With 1 player chosen in the menu, *Practice* now pits your knight against a computer-played Black Knight; with 2 or more it is two players, as before (1000:011D, 1000:2031; needs `SAVEHOOK.COM`). | emulator-tested; not yet tried in play |
| D12 | **Practice for 3 or 4 players, in two teams** (a prototype). With 3 or 4 players chosen, *Practice* adds knights 3 and 4 on the game controllers that answer (1, then 2), then on the third key set (I J K L, B, N; 0.7.0), as many as the players chosen and the inputs allow: team 1 is player 1 (arrows) with controller 1, team 2 is player 2 (W A S D) with controller 2. The keyboard and the controllers are then four separate inputs, teammates do not hurt each other, and the fight ends when one team has no knight left. With 4 players and no controller, three knights. Needs `SAVEHOOK.COM`. | tested (3 and 4 players, two PS3 controllers) |
| D13 | **Four colours in that practice.** Every knight has its own colour: player 1 blue, player 2 green, controller 1 orange, controller 2 red; with custom rules, the colours of `[Knight1_blue]`, `[Knight3_green]`, `[Knight2_orange]` and `[Knight4_red]`. The game uses only 32 of the screen's 256 palette entries; the controller knights' armour is moved to entries 32-37 as they are drawn, and those entries follow the game's fades. No extra sprite files or memory. Needs `SAVEHOOK.COM`. | tested |
| D14 | **Practice choices.** 3-4 players: Enter on *Practice* = everyone against everyone (four colours, until one knight is left), Space anywhere in the first menu = two teams in their keyboard players' colours (with 1-2 players Space starts the normal practice). 1 player: the keys 1-9 start a practice against the monsters of one lair through the game's own lair fight (several at once, as in the lair), 0 the Black Knight (1000:1299, 0142; needs `SAVEHOOK.COM`). | emulator-tested (menu keys, set-up calls, end rules); play test pending |
| D15 | **Co-op practice against monsters.** With 2-4 players chosen, the practice keys put all the knights on the same side: they cannot hurt each other (thrown daggers neither), each has its own colour, every monster goes for the knight nearest to it, and the fight goes on while a knight stands and monsters are left. Before each object's update (1000:98FD) `SAVEHOOK.COM` makes the knight the monsters' moves read ([0978h]) that monster's nearest knight. The fight start and each knight's walk-in toggled every monster's pause (1000:025C), so with 2 or 4 knights the monsters never moved; now only knight 1's walk-in toggles. A knight that fell no longer gets up again when the others win (the end restores the knight in [8979h], now one standing). Esc ends any practice fight. A ratman hit by a knight took the damage of the knight it was going for, not of the one that hit it (1000:34A7, 34C7; the same knight when alone) (needs `SAVEHOOK.COM`). | emulator-tested (set-up, targeting, pause, Esc, end rule, hits, colours); play test pending |
| D16 | **Practice settings.** In `custom_rules.ini`, `[Practice]`: each key 1-0 starts the monsters of a chosen lair (1-24; type and number as the lair has them) or the Black Knight; whether knights' blows and daggers hurt other knights; whether monsters go for the nearest knight or the first, and when they switch. With 2-4 knights against monsters, their strength and hit points are added up when the game counts the monsters (custom rules; needs `SAVEHOOK.COM`, no change in `MAIN.EXE`). | emulator-tested; play test pending |
| D19 | **Black Knights against 2-4 knights.** The 0 key's Black Knight practice with 2-4 players: the knights together against as many Black Knights (each a copy of one knight), every one going for the knight nearest to it; they do not hurt each other. The B key: the same with strong black ones (strength, constitution, endurance 5, Sword of Sharpness, battle armour), also with one player. The fight is a lair fight whose monsters are computer knights (`1000:2824`, `0243`, `A14C`). |
| D20 | **New deaths.** A player's knight slumped on its knees after a deadly blow (`1000:130E`) and hit again gets a death by that blow: up strike: split in two; down and forward thrust: through the middle of the body (front or back); back strike: down through the helmet; up and forward strike: lifted on the sword; a dagger: stays where it hit (face, back of the head, belly or back). Also from Black Knights, axe troggs (split) and ratmen (thrust). The computer's Black Knights get them too (0.7.0): the purple one of a one-player practice (a knight slot) and the black ones of the B key and BLACKDEATH (monster slots), when drawn from KN3.OB or HE3.OB. `1000:2D5E` picks the death (int 63h); the pieces are added to KN3.OB and the scripts written into the data segment (DS:F480) by `death_frames.py`; the knights' bank area grows by 200h paragraphs (`86A7`; SAVEHOOK.COM is loaded high so DOS has the room), and with EMS the cached files after KN3.OB and HE3.OB move on by 1600h (DS:828A). A duel's second knight (HE3.OB) gets them too. The striker goes on with these blows (`4159`), as the game did only with the sweep. |
| D21 | **Dragon debug mode** (custom rules `dragon_debug_mode = 1`, off by default). On the map the keys 1-4 make knight 1-4 the dragon's target, and Enter by the knight whose turn it is starts the dragon fight when it stands under the dragon. In the game the dragon attacks only its own target, when it flies over it in that knight's turn (`1000:A5BB`), and Enter only runs a place's action (`B000`). `mov di,6E26h` at `1000:A5BB` becomes `int 63h`. | from the code |
| D22 | **Gore off without blood** (the game's blood option off; until D76 also `death_animations = off`). Pieces made only of blood (colour 15) are not drawn in a fight, and the blood on the knights, the Balok and the boar takes the colour of the pixel beside it (the draw and recolour hooks; not the dragon, whose own colours use 15). The troll's blow (`1000:43B0`) and the Balok's leap (`3729`) give the plain death (`1310`) instead of the crush (`16E2`). D20 is off. Every hit shows a hit marker, a white X of three frames (`KN3.OB`, script `DS:FE60`), made at the hit test (`1000:A14C`) where the two boxes meet. | from the code |
| D23 | **Faster drawing.** The converter call (`1000:5EDA`, `call cs:[638Fh + mask * 2]`) goes to SAVEHOOK.COM, which turns the frame's planes into pixels with a 2 KB table (a byte's bits as 8 bytes) and 386 registers, one routine per plane mask the game has a converter for (others go to the game's). The knights' recolour (D13/D15) uses a translate table. Same output as the game's, byte for byte (every mask with random data; whole fights from saves, screen and memory compared); a fight about a third fewer instructions. The two knight pieces with 1-3 planes (KN3.OB 31, KN4.OB 25), which the recolour never reached, now take the knight's colours. | from the code |
| D24 | **Responsive controls.** The game reads its key table once a frame (2 BIOS ticks) and a knight reads its input only when its move is over (`1000:98F6`), so short taps and attacks pressed during a move were lost. The keyboard interrupt's store (`8271`) goes to SAVEHOOK.COM (int 64h), which keeps a release back until the key has been down 3 ticks (let through at the key test, `828B`); a fire key pressed in the last 8 ticks gives the knight that attack when its move ends (`3F16`, with the directions noted; `input_buffer`, on by default). Players 1 and 2 on the keyboard (and pads mapped to keys). A back direction without fire waits `input_chord` ticks (2) before it turns the knight (`3F16`), so back + up or back + down + fire keeps the facing; a direction let go up to 3 ticks before fire counts with it; `input_log` writes INPUT.LOG (`tools/input_log.py`). | tested in Unicorn, not played |
| D25 | **Turning in place, walking up close.** A knight's turn (`1000:3F83`, `3F92`) mirrors its pose about its x, which the standing knight is not centred on (x-13..x+23), so it hopped 10 sideways and stepped at once; now x moves to keep the body in place and that frame has no step. A step blocked by another figure's box (`9FDF`; the boxes are the drawn frames' extents, the steps 25, 3, 23, 4) is cut to the gap (`3FAC`), so figures come up to touching. An attack with up or down and left or right within 5 ticks of a turn is read with the facing from before it and undoes the turn (`40AC`): back + up throws the dagger. | from the code |
| D26 | **A new turn waits for the keys to be let go.** A player still holding a direction (or fire) as the turn changed moved the next knight on the map at once. At a human's turn start (D7, `1000:A63F`) the map's input (`1000:A536`) stays empty until no direction or fire is held; then the next press moves the knight. | not yet tried in play |
| D27 | **Rules as in the manual** (custom rules; not in speedrun mode). Healer: the first 10 gp heal, then 15 gp per life up to `max_lives`; what cannot buy a whole one is a chance (4 gp: 40 % for the heal; 27 gp: heal, a life and 2/15 for another); money nothing can use is returned (`BCAC`). The home village always restores full hit points. Cursed Scroll of Haste: half movement (`haste_curse_chance`, `CCCF`). Cursed Scroll of the Wyrm: the dragon goes for the reader (`wyrm_curse_chance`, `C03C`). The Scroll of the Hawk cannot enter the Valley of the Gods (`A568`, `AB7D`). The Scroll of Protection also works against the dragon: it flies off and the turn ends (`0D15`). | emulated |
| D28 | **Low-HP flicker for all knights.** The game flickers only two knights at 10 HP or less. In practices with new colours (D13, D15) the other knights' colours now flash white as well (`0371`). Replaced by D60's pulse. | emulated |
| D29 | **Installation integrity** (0.8.0). `rules.swap_files` changes the programs as one step, with undo; `INSTALLING.FLG` blocks `Start.bat` and `Fixes_On.bat` after a change that did not finish, and `SETUP.FLG`, which only a finished Setup removes, blocks them and `Custom_rules_on.bat` after a Setup that did not; `Start.bat` compares MAIN.EXE and SAVEHOOK.COM with `_fixes/rules`; Setup checks the age rating before its first write; a failed music fallback stops the start. `Fixes_On.bat`, `Fixes_Off.bat` and `Custom_rules_off.bat` switch through `tools/mode.py`, so their files and flags (ORIGINAL.FLG, CUSTOM_RULES.FLG; `swap_files` also removes) change as one step too. Setup, the custom rules and the switches share a lock (`_fixes/UPDATE.LCK`, `rules.install_lock`, released by the system if a process dies) and stage under names of their own, so two changes in one folder never mix. A finished change writes `BUILD.TXT` (release, source revision, tools hash, mode, hashes of the programs and generated files) for playtest reports. | tested (`tools/test_review_fixes.py`), not played |
| D30 | **Save check and fate streams** (0.8.0). Only DOS error 2 on `SAVEHOOK.CHK` starts a new token; other open, read, write and close failures and a short file refuse. The fate streams are seeded at knight set-up (`1000:1FEA`), read no shared state later, and never store a zero. | tested in Unicorn, not played |
| D31 | **Swords in their own tints** (0.8.0; Rob Anderson's wish for visible weapons). The sword is always drawn from KN4.OB (blade 1-2, hilt 3-4). For a knight (+35h = 6 or 8) the draw hook (`9AFD`, `9B09`) sets a shift by its weapon (+40h) that the recolour (`5EDF`) adds to those four pixel values: long sword entries 48-51 (blackened steel, black iron grip), broadsword 52-55 (bronze), Sword of Sharpness 56-59 (blue-white, gold hilt); the claymore keeps the game's steel. A piece of a knight from its body files (KN1-3.OB, KN5.OB, HE1-3.OB; its greys 1-4) by its armour (+42h): chain mail entries 60-63 (brass), plate mail 64-67 (cool blue-grey iron), battle armour 68-71 (blackened steel, gold trim); padded armour keeps the game's steel. The palette write and both fades write 48-71 too. | tested in Unicorn, not played |
| D33 | **Co-op lairs on the map** (in progress; custom rules `coop_lairs` full / shared / off, full by default). The map's list of choices (`1000:06B5`, `DS:043C`, 5 entries with the end) is redone at its end (`08CA`): the other human knights listed beside a lair (type 1, `+20h` <> 4, up to 3, as many as the inputs allow) are the partners, one entry (type 40h) "Lair with" + a name or "Lair together (N knights)"; with 2 or more knights to fight and 2-3 partners or no room those become one "Battle with ..." (type 41h). Texts by `OrderOpt` (`B137`). `StackDecision` (`B089`): "Lair with" goes on as the lair's own fight with the partners; "Battle with ..." puts the knights to fight in the list and goes back to `DisplayStack` (`B000`). The lair's set-up (`058B`) runs as the co-op practice's: all the knights' strength and HP for the monsters, then each partner set up (`1F76`, x 220 / 280 / 190) and registered (`2998`); `knight_list` and `coop` = 1 + the partners. Inputs: the visit's knight's own, each partner's own unless taken, else the first free of player 1's keys, player 2's, joystick A, joystick B and the third key set; a knight on a joystick keeps it from being merged with the key sets (`joy_own`, `83F0`, `8422`). After it (`0597`) each partner's HP and lives as the game does for its knight, its own fields back; won while one stands. The chest (`05C6`, `BFAC` with ax = 2; the item screen's knight is `[8979h]`, `D492`) opens for every knight standing: `full` in a drawn order, the gold and items put back for each (not the keys or Moonstones); `shared` the visit's knight first, then a drawn order, one chest grown by `extra_loot_per_knight`, a click on the gold (`CED3`, its loop `CEFA`) taking the gold / the knights, the last one all. Each knight is recoloured by its colour slot (`+20h`). Every hit counts (friendly fire). | tested in Unicorn; played: the fight (2 knights) |

| D46 | **Timed block, dodge and counters** (0.8.0, custom rules, `timed_defence`) | A block (move 8) or dodge (move Eh) counts only if it started after the attack's animation (`td_atk`, noted by `td_note_attack` at 2D5E) and within `defence_window` ticks of its own start; a hold does not restart it. `td_move` (40CA) notes the start and picks the fast scripts: a bonus swing at 0EAAh, the backward thrust always at 0DB6h, a lunge against a facing chop still in the air (riposte, `td_foe_chop`) at 102Ah. The fast swing does a lunge's base damage and the riposte a backward thrust's (`td_fk`, `td_fast_dmg` from the damage hook). `td_check` (441F, 4D6C) after CheckBlock's answer (`td_kind`, `td_judge`): swing + block in time: no damage, the attacker staggers (`td_stagger`, `td_cut`: script C90h with gore on, as 128Eh's frames have blood; the hit reaction with gore off; no input for `stagger_time`), `fast_swing_time` for the fast swing; too early: a fifth, at least 1, with the ordinary hit reaction (`td_adjust` from 44C2, 4DA1). Thrust + dodge in time: misses and the attacker staggers, which also ends its later weapon frames; a chop dodged in time just misses. An attack's start is noted only when the move's own script (or a fast start) begins, not when a struck or hit-something end keeps the old move. `td_collide` (from hit_pair) cuts a chop in the air when another blow lands first. `td_quiet` (hit_pair): no marker or extra blood on a human knight defending in time. Daggers cannot be stopped. Axe and spear troggs: `td_monster` (42D8, 432A) within 6 ticks of the block's start, axe's late hit a fifth (`td_trogg`, 4302), spear's in full. Smart computer knights: one roll per attack at the first contact (`td_cpu`, `td_roll`, so a defended blow makes no marker; a defence rolled succeeds) (`purple_knight_*` for the four knight slots, `black_knight_*` for monster slots), halved against thrusts and chops, `computer_defence_pause` attacks without defence after a good one, half the stagger, no fast swing; BKBlock's stance (4C93) and D45's block go to the roll; they step away from or wait out a foe's chop in the air (`bkt_chop`), and monsters do not walk under one (`mon_walk`), with `anticheese` = yes (with the older anti-cheese rules) / blackknights_only / no. The P key in the first menu: a white practice knight (`dummy`, `DUMMY_WHITE`) that defends, always in time, against a chop ripostes half the time (`bkd_on`), and attacks on its own after 10 s without an attack from its foe (counted from when it comes on the screen), then every 4-6 s, in turn a swing, a lunge, a chop (`dm_attack`, `dm_wait`). A staggered knight's stagger script plays on for the whole stagger (`td_stag_script`, from `td_hold` and bk_track). The dragon: TOOEASY's no-flinch rule is gone; with anticheese = yes a 4th blow in a row without the dragon hurting the knight may end in its bite (50 %, 80 % from the 5th; `dragon_hit` at 3AB4 to 3AF3), and the long fire's head follows the knight's depth only up to a random 10-69, leaving an edge clear when begun near it (`dragon_long` 389C, `dragon_depth` 39A6). Classic knights keep the game's block. A knight whose extended (F480h-FE50h) death has ended stays down: `knight_down` and `bk_down` (4B85). The 3-4 knight practice: a knight's death is no longer drawn into the background in the first colours (knights 3 and 4 lay blue, then their own again). Missing from an old ini: off. |

A knight in its dodge (move Eh) is never hit, whatever the timing (since 0.9.0: `td_check` at 441F/4D6C sets `[77FCh]` 1 before D46's timing, a computer knight's too), and Daggerward (D37) still avoids it; a dagger from the sky (D49) is unchanged. | D65 | **The Amiga version's sound effects** (0.9.0; optional, needs the user's own three Amiga disk images) | `tools/make_amiga_audio.py` reads the Amiga sound driver's tables from `mog` (disk 1) and the sample banks (disks 2 and 3), steps each of the 140 effect programs as the driver does (50 steps a second: instrument, note, volume, slices, rests) through a model of a Paula channel, and writes the results as one sound per key into `_music\amiga\amiga.sf2` (`tools/soundfont.py`; programs 1-100 are keys 20-119 of preset 0, 101-166 keys 20-85 of preset 1). Nothing from the disks is in the release. In the game `cmp word [8645h],3` at the start of the sound routine (`1000:5979`) is int 63h: when `AMIGASND.FLG` is there (Start.bat makes it while `music.conf` is the profile `_music\profiles\amiga.conf`: `mididevice = synth`, the SoundFont as `midiconfig`) and the sound id has a program (`amiga_prog`; the id is the Amiga's program number, the DOS menu click 15 is program 156, Moonstone Rebuilt's own ids 171-172 have none), SAVEHOOK.COM sends "UART mode, program change, note on" to port 330h on the next of four MIDI channels and skips the Sound Blaster. Every sound is in one exclusive class, so a new note on a channel fades out the sound that channel was playing: four voices, as on the Amiga. A program that loops by itself is played once round (a real loop since D67). `amiga_check` reads the flag when SAVEHOOK starts and at every automatic save, so a loaded state follows the installation. Found on the way: of DOSBox-X's two FluidSynth devices only `synth` plays through the mixer (`fluidsynth` uses its own audio output), `synth` needs a program change before the first note, and its FluidSynth (1.1.6) plays samples whose root key is under 15 too low. GUNSHOT's bang has its own id 172 now (it used 62, the Amiga's wind). | emulator-tested; the SoundFont and the MIDI messages checked in DOSBox-X by capturing its output; not yet listened to in the game |
| D66 | **The Amiga version's sound effects without the Amiga disks** (0.9.0; optional, `AmigaSound_On.bat`) | D65 needed the user's three Amiga disk images. Now Setup builds the same SoundFont without them. `tools/sound_programs.json` holds each of the 140 effect programs as what the Amiga's driver plays frame by frame: steps of [frames of 1/50 s, volume 0-63, sample rate in Hz, start (an instrument, or -1 where the sound is cut)], recorded from the driver by `tools/export_sound_programs.py` (`record` in `make_amiga_audio.py`; `Replay` plays the steps through the same model of a Paula channel). It has no samples, no period table and no program code of the Amiga game. The table also says where each of the 112 instruments comes from: 68 are a DOS sample or a part of one, byte for byte the Amiga's data with the sign turned (the dragon's four grunts are parts of `DRAGRU`, the beast's impale is 3,338 samples of `RATMPAL` from 235); 23 are our own substitute sounds in `sounds/` (14 files: nine synthesized by `tools/make_substitute_sounds.py`, five cut from CC0 recordings by `tools/make_recorded_sounds.py`; all CC0 1.0, see `sounds/SOURCES.md`); 9 play another take of the same sound that DOS has (`stand_in`); 12 are silent (the instruments of the location music, the healer's sample, a synth wave used at volume 0). `build_soundfont` uses the disk images if all three are in `amiga`, else the installation's `SAMPLES` folder and `sounds/`. Eight programs are silent without the disks (115, 157, 161-166) and are left out of the SoundFont. | checked by a development test (needs the disk images): replaying the record gives the driver's samples for all 140 programs, and the 88 programs whose samples are all in the DOS game come out sample for sample as from the disks; the SoundFont built from the disks through the new code is the same file as before. Not yet listened to: the 44 programs with substitutes or stand-ins; the five recordings were chosen by the user by ear from three candidates each (12 Nov 2026) |
| D67 | **The Amiga sound effects stop with the fight, and the loops loop** (0.9.1; part of the optional Amiga sound effects, D65) | SAVEHOOK.COM now handles the four channels as the Amiga game does (its routines `02A440`-`02A4C8` and `02A55A`). **Channels of their own:** the Amiga plays three looping sounds on a named channel and reserves it: the dragon's breath (29, channel 0, at `016D28`), its fire (27, channel 1, `016D32`) and the mudmen's mud (80, channel 0, `03CBBC`). `amiga_fixed` lists them; `amiga_reserved` has a bit for each reserved channel. Every other sound takes the next channel that is not reserved, and is not played when all four are. **Stop:** `amiga_stop` sends controller 120 ("all sound off") on the four channels and clears the reservations. It runs at the end of a fight (in D17's handler at `1000:0ABE`; the Amiga calls `02A55A` at the same place, `010804`) and after the automatic save's wait at the start of a fight (D6, `1000:0307`) and of a human knight's turn on the map (D7), which is also where a loaded automatic save goes on. Nothing is sent while the Amiga effects are off, because the MIDI port may then be the music's. **Giving a channel back:** the DOS game still has the Amiga's calls for it. The dragon's three scripts call `1000:5833` (the game's own names `KAudio0`/`KAudio1`; an empty `ret` in DOS, the Amiga's `02A480` and `02A492`) after the breath (`DS:41CA`, `DS:450C`) and after the fire (`DS:4946`). The three calls now go to `1000:3E32`, an unused `ret` that is now int 63h: `loop_release` gives channels 0 and 1 back if they are reserved and silences them. This differs from the Amiga on purpose: there the release plays the silent program 167 on the next channel in turn, which is often not the loop's, so the breath or the fire sounds on until another sound happens to take its channel. **Loops in the SoundFont:** a program that begins again by itself is a looped sample (`record_program` and `font_sound` in `tools/make_amiga_audio.py`). The driver's restart command goes back to the start of the program that was asked for, with the transposing left as it is. So 27, 29, 34, 62, 78, 79 and 80 are the same every round and loop from their start; a Balok's roars 35-42 (a roar, then the den sound of 34) are lower from the second round on, so their first round is played once and the second is the loop; 134 rises a step every round, never repeats and is played once. The round that repeats is rendered twice and its second time is the sample, so its end runs into its start. A loop never starts at its sample's first sample: the FluidSynth in DOSBox-X (1.1.6, `fixup_sample`) takes such a loop as broken and loops from 8 samples in to 8 from the end, which made every round 48 samples (1.09 ms) too long and repeated a bit of its start; so a loop with nothing before it (27, 29, 34, 62, 78, 79, 80) has its own last 64 samples in front of it. `tools/sound_programs.json` has a new field `loop` (the step the program goes back to), and a looping program's steps are now exactly its rounds (they had one frame of the next round). **Without the disk images the lions' den is a new sound, played in one piece.** The Amiga plays its den sample as eight parts of 1,034 samples back and forth, a new part every 0.12 s; its sample is a noise-like rumble that hides the joins. The first substitute (0.9.0) was a lion's moan cut the same way: heard as a loop it clicked at every join, had a pitch, and was 14 dB louder than the Amiga's sample (RMS 0.257 against 0.050), because every substitute was brought to full scale. Now `sounds/lion_den.wav` is 9.24 s of another recording (a lion growling low and breathing; CC0, see `sounds/SOURCES.md`), its loud moments at RMS 0.52 of full scale with the highest peaks rounded off (a level the user set by ear in the game in three steps: in DOSBox-X the den then plays 12 dB under the roar) and in 16 bits, and `merged` in `tools/export_sound_programs.py` turns each run of the den's notes into one note of 462 frames that plays it from its start (`CONTINUOUS`). So in the table 34 is one note that loops, and 35-42 are a roar, the den, the lower roar, the den, with the loop at the lower roar. With the disk images the den is the Amiga's own, in its eight parts. The SoundFont is 27 MB without the disk images (was 20) and 29 MB from them (was 22). The den and the Guardian's sparks (78, from a script) loop on a channel of the turn, as on the Amiga: until the fourth sound after them takes the channel or the fight ends. **Not heard:** nothing in the DOS game asks for the wind (62), 79 or 134. | tested in Unicorn (the MIDI bytes of each case, `tests/test_amiga_loops.py`) and by a capture from DOSBox-X with the real SoundFont: the fire loop played 12 s (2.5 rounds), its second round the same as the first (correlation 1.0000, 4.8 s after it to within a sample of the capture; 1.1 ms late before the loops got samples in front of them), silent within 30 ms of "all sound off"; the den the same over its 9.24 s round; a sword clash on the fire's channel cut the loop. The loops match the driver for three rounds sample for sample (development test, needs the disk images). Not yet listened to in the game |
| D68 | **The dragon's fire can be dodged in depth** (0.9.1; custom rules, `anticheese = yes`; user report: in the top or bottom right corner the long fire always caught a knight running from it, above all downwards from the top) | Measured by running the game's fight loop (1000:0351-036F) in Unicorn from a DOSBox-X save of the D practice. **The ground:** on the dragon's ground a border (`SBORD` 455E, DS:88FFh: x 0-319, y 129) keeps knights from depth 82 down, not 21, so it is 82-146 deep and D46's "near the top" (21 + 40) never held. `dd_reach` now takes the top from the ground's borders at the knight's x (`dd_top`; at most 32 entries, else 21) and "near an edge" is 20. **The long fire** (the knight 71-95 away): D46's reach held only for the head's 13 ticks (3985); after them `DragonAttack` (39E6) makes a flame every third tick at the dragon's depth + 5 (`AddDragonFIRE` 3B22) and the walk (3BF4, `56E5`) took the dragon 5 a tick to the knight's depth (3C5D, 3C66). `dragon_walk` (3C6A) keeps the flames' depth within the reach while it lasts (the knight more than 70 away; nearer, the claw and bite follow as in the game). **The far fire** (96 or more away, the corners): attack 4, script 4092, rears the head for six frames, shows the fire across the ground from the seventh and hurt a knight in its path then (frame 7), which left two frames (0.2 s) from the first sign to start running: in the emulator a knight that ran at frame 3 was always hit. At the top the dragon comes level from below (no nearer than its +56h, 10), and a step down was refused: its box takes in the fire across the ground, so 9FDF (`CheckZAxis` A0CE, `CheckAboveBelow` A082) and D25's own scans (`walk_block` 3FAC) cut every step towards it, while the border held the knight from above. Now: `dragon_far` (3A6B, each far fire's start) takes a reach as above and the time (`dd_t`); `dragon_walk` keeps the dragon's depth within it while +28h is 4; `dragon_pass` (A014, the call of A022 in 9FDF's loop; also `far_dragon` in `walk_block`'s scans) lets no step be cut by the dragon in its far fire; `dragon_burn` (A15A, TaskCol's mov [si+0Ch],di) registers no hit by it before its 14th frame (`FAR_SPARE`, 2 BIOS ticks a frame, 98BA). In the emulator a knight at the top or the bottom edge that starts running up to 9-10 frames after the far fire begins (3-4 frames, about 0.35 s, after the fire shows) gets away, one that stands is burnt at frame 15 (before: at frame 7, and only a start within 2 frames got away). The fight's start (D6's int 66h) clears the reach. `anticheese = no` and HOTSTUFF's dragon as in the game | emulator-tested (Unicorn suite and the fight-loop runs, `research/dragon_fire`); not yet tried in play |

| D70 | **Three players on one keyboard** (0.9.1; user request, 13 Nov 2026) | With 3 or 4 players chosen, players 1-3 keep a key set of their own: player 1 the arrows + Enter (right Shift), player 2 W A S D + Tab (left Shift), player 3 I J K L + B (N), the third key set of D12. The game gave a fight's keys by position: every knight alone in a lair on the arrows (`ChooseFIRE` 16CA sets `+9 = 2`), a knight-against-knight fight the arrows to `[8979h]` and W A S D to `[897Bh]` (`Combat` 03FF-041C). | D82 | **General MIDI music with the Amiga sound effects** (0.9.1; part of the optional Amiga sound effects, D65; user's choices, 14 Nov 2026) | Before, the two could not be on together. The GM/Roland tune player (`ATUNE`/`RTUNE`, int 60h) uses the MPU-401's intelligent mode: each message is a "want to send data" (D0h + channel to 331h, at `02BD`; `02D3` in `ATUNE5`, a build shifted by 16h), and the player then waits for the acknowledge FEh with no time-out, inside the timer interrupt. SAVEHOOK.COM's effects switch the MPU to UART mode (3Fh) before every note, after which DOSBox-X ignores the commands: the next tick would wait for ever (shown by `research/sf2_spike/make_spike11.py` in DOSBox-X: the original module froze after one hook note; the patched one does not). The tunes have no running status and both sides run with interrupts off, so nothing else conflicts. **Tunes:** `make_gm_tunes.py` also writes a UART set, `_music\gm_amiga` (and D8's muted intro and ending copies in `_musicmiga\gm_amiga`): the command routine, found by its bytes in all six modules, becomes "3Fh to 331h, read 330h, ret" (the acknowledge in intelligent mode, ignored in UART mode), and the start's "reset all controllers" and volume 127 cover channels 0-9 instead of 0-15 (`cmp ch,10h` -> `0Ah`). The GM and MT-32 sets stay byte for byte. **SAVEHOOK.COM:** the effects' four slots (the Amiga's channels, `amiga_reserved`'s bits) play on MIDI channels 12-15 (`AMIGA_FX_CHANNEL`; the tunes use 1-6 and 9), and a bank select (controller 0 = `AMIGA_BANK` 100) goes before every program change, for the effects (`sp_send`) and for the location music (`mus_part`); `amiga_stop` and `loop_release` silence 12-15. `music_quiet` (was D73's and D74's loop) sets all sound off, bank 0 and volume 100 on 4-7, so a GM tune after an Amiga piece finds bank 0 again and an Amiga piece after a GM start (volume 127) plays at its usual level. The same bytes serve with or without GM music: no new setting. **SoundFonts:** `soundfont.write` puts the Amiga presets in bank 100, also in the plain `amiga.sf2`. `tools/merge_soundfont.py` joins the GM font (the user's `soundfonts\default.sf2`, else the shipped GeneralUser GS 2.0.3) with `amiga.sf2` into `_musicmigamiga_gm.sf2` (DOSBox-X's `synth` device takes one file: two names in `midiconfig` gave silence): the sample data joined with 46 zero samples, every index rebased, a first-font preset in the second font's bank and program dropped, SF3 and 24-bit fonts refused, INFO kept with a note added. It is made again when an input changes (`amiga_gm.sf2.inputs`). At the effects' mixer level (600) tune 1 clipped (peak 1.77), so every GM preset gets 6 dB more attenuation (the user's choice by ear), `GM_ATTENUATION = 150`: FluidSynth takes 0.4 of a generator's attenuation (measured: 60 gave 2.40 dB, 150 gave 6.00 dB), and a preset zone's own attenuation replaces the global zone's, so both are raised. The `synth` device has reverb and chorus, but the `fluid.*` lines do not reach it, so the new profile `amiga_gm.conf` has none. **Switches:** `tools/set_sound.bat` composes `music.conf` from the music (read from `music.conf`) and the effects (`effects.cfg`, `amiga` or `dos`; without it, from `music.conf`), copies the tune set and makes the merged font; the `Music_*.bat` and `AmigaSound_*.bat` files call it, each keeping the other choice (MT-32 and the Amiga effects exclude each other, with a message), and `Start.bat` calls it to fall back and makes `AMIGASND.FLG` from `effects.cfg`. **GeneralUser GS 2.0.3** ships unchanged in `soundfonts` with its licence; Setup checks its SHA-256 and builds the UART set and the merged font. Tests: `tests/test_gm_uart.py`, `tests/test_merge_soundfont.py`, the amiga suites' bytes, the regression's `SoundSwitches`; a DOSBox-X capture with the installed files (tune 1 6.00 dB down, its shape the same, the effects at their level, no hang, no clipping); the autosave check with the new profile |

| D87 | **The Amiga intro and ending music from the SoundFont, in step with the pictures** (unreleased; replaces D8's host player, which is gone since 15 Nov 2026; needs the user's own Amiga disk 1; user's listening and targets, 14 Nov 2026) | D8 played WAV files with Windows from the moment INTR.EXE started, 40 s before the intro starts its tune (`008B`), and the DOS pictures are faster than the Amiga's: at the first lightning the music was 19 s ahead. **Player:** `tools/amiga_module.py` steps the two modules (`music.cmp`, `vmusic.cmp`, tag FLT4) as the routine in the Amiga's `program` file does (hunk 1, `011250`-`0119E4`): 50 ticks a second, commands 0-4 and A, B, C, D, F; D ignores its parameter, F is the speed only, C writes the volume register only, a looped sample ends at its loop's end, each sample's first long is cleared. The routine's table of periods is read from the user's disk, found by a checksum. One play-through is 104.64 s and 97.92 s; the note starts agree with VLC's render to 0.4 ms. **SoundFont:** `make_amiga_audio.py` (`tune_sides`, through the `Paula` class and the Amiga 500 filter) stores each tune as a left (Paula channels 0 + 3) and a right (1 + 2) sample, keys 40-43 of preset 3 in bank 100, looped whole, release 0.3 s, whenever disk 1 is in `amiga\`, and writes `amiga.tunes` (`amiga_gm.tunes` for the merged font). Every older sound is unchanged and the build without disks is byte for byte as before. A Paula channel is 0.75 of full scale there (two are summed) and the notes play at MIDI volume 113, the Amiga's proportion to the effects. **Tune module:** `make_amiga_music.py` writes `_music\amiga\font\ATUNE1.BIN` and `ATUNE6.BIN`, int 60h handlers of 303 bytes that INTR.EXE loads in place of its tunes (first byte 1Eh, as K9 expects): ah = 0 (only when the tune is not playing already) sends 3Fh to 331h and, on MIDI channels 10 and 11, bank 100, program 3, volume, pan (16 and 111) and the two note-ons; ah = 2 the note-offs. **Start:** the Amiga starts its intro tune in the first sequence, when its stage first reaches 4 (`program` `0296A2`, after 19 of about 214 passes); DOS has the same test there (`0D76`) and calls an empty routine (`08EB`), and starts its tune later, at `008B`. K9's patch now replaces that test and call with a far call to the cave (`0492:234C`, relocated), which does the test and, only when our module is loaded (the word 13EBh at `0492:0001`), starts the tune there (`int 60h`, ah = 0; the timer and the vector are set at `0935` and the module is loaded at `005F`, both before): 20.4 s earlier than before, 19.7 s after INTR.EXE starts. The start at `008B` then does nothing. With a DOS tune the test, the stage and the tune's start are as in the game (only the empty call is gone). The ending's start (`058F`) is where the Amiga's is (`0109AA`, after the same picture routine) and stays. **Switches:** `tools/set_sound.bat` decides (`SOUND_AMIGA_TUNES`) and `Start.bat` copies the modules to DISKA when `AMIGA.FLG` is there, `ORIGINAL.FLG` is not (the original INTR.EXE has no pace), the music is AdLib or General MIDI and the font in use has its marker: with the Amiga effects as before; with the DOS effects AdLib music uses the `amiga` profile (DOSBox-X loads `amiga.sf2` for the tunes alone; SAVEHOOK sends no effects without `AMIGASND.FLG`) and General MIDI the `amiga_gm` one with the UART tune set, as with the Amiga effects (made by `merge_soundfont.py`; without Python GM music keeps the fluidsynth device and its DOS tunes). A profile that no longer fits (the switch, the font or original mode changed) is written again at the next start, tune sets included. `AMIGATUN.FLG` (from `Start.bat`) gives the synth its mixer level of 600 also without the Amiga effects (`moonstone-dosbox-x.conf`). MT-32 music keeps its Roland tunes. A font module left in DISKA is replaced by the set's DOS tune first. **The host player retired** (phase 5 step 7, 15 Nov 2026): `amiga_music.ps1`, the WAV files, the muted tune sets and the VLC step are gone, and `make_amiga_music.py` removes what D8 left in an install (`intro.mod`, `ending.mod`, `intro.wav`, `ending.wav`, `amiga_music.log`, `_music\amiga\adlib`, `gm`, `gm_amiga`, `mt32`). `Setup.bat` installs numpy into `_python` when the `amiga` folder holds a disk image (the font, and so the tunes, need it). `AmigaMusic_On.bat` and `AmigaMusic_Off.bat` set the profile at once (`set_sound.bat keep keep`). **Pace:** INTR.EXE times its pictures by vertical retraces (`0F9E`, `0FAE`) and, in the scenes' frame loops (`0129`, `0162`, `0694`), by two ticks of the BIOS clock (`021F`, `022F`); the Amiga waits 10 or 12 frames a pass there (its `$11c2e + $14a8a`, which DOS still writes to `[157Dh]` and `[4105h]` and never reads). While our module plays (known by the short jump after its `push ds`), K9's cave makes n of every m retrace waits wait once more, and the module's tick (INTR.EXE's 54.6 Hz timer calls it, `09F1`) puts INTR.EXE's count to the next BIOS tick (`[12B9h]`) back up on `skip` of every `of` ticks, so the BIOS clock runs slower. The module's head holds two such paces and a tick count: the start puts the first in use, the tick the second after that many ticks. The numbers are set by measurement (video captures in DOSBox-X at 4000 cycles, the tune's start found from the capture's sound). With the earlier start the DOS pace brings the intro's first lightning 0.3 s before its thunder by itself, and the second scene's bright flashes 5.1 s too early; so the intro has no pace until 3223 ticks (59.0 s, just after the first flash), then 2 of 5 retraces and 18 of 47 ticks (1.62 times slower): the first bright flash of the two lightning scenes at 58.67 s and 81.33 s of the tune (targets 58.7 and 81.4, 0.3 s before the thunder at 59.04 and 81.72 s); the intro's pictures end at 95.0 s of the tune (its last sound 95.3 s), 9 s later than the DOS game's (tune 104.6 s). The ending has no such mark; it keeps 7 of 16 ticks, a little less, so that its pictures (96.6 s) end before its tune (97.9 s) comes round. A skipped intro stops the tune through the same stop. | D94 | **The game hung at the end of a fight** (unreleased; SAVEHOOK.COM: the speed change with custom rules, D71's menu check also without; user report with saves 3 and 4, 15 Nov 2026: a won lair fight hung before the chest screen, a lost one before the knight's item screen) | Every fight's end (D17's `rules_fight_end`, 1000:0ABE) runs the speed change: SAVEHOOK.COM freed its 4 KB reserve (`RESERVE_PARAS` 100h) and ran `Z:\COMMAND.COM /C CONFIG -set cycles=… >NUL`. D92 made D38's bank area (1000:869E, the block at 597Ah) 80h paragraphs bigger, which took the last free 7Fh above the reserve, so the child had only the reserve: COMMAND.COM (its copy of the environment 49h, itself 40h) left CONFIG.COM 2Bh paragraphs at 9FD4h. DOSBox-X's program stub needs 40h (1 KB); its stack ended up beyond the block in the video memory at A000h, and with the game's latched VGA writes (write mode 1) the return address read back as garbage. Both saves show it: the int 21h exec from `no_free` on SAVEHOOK's stack, CONFIG's PSP at 9FD4h with the tail `-set cycles=4000`, the CPU at 1B12:1B14 with ss = ds = 9FD4h and the trap flag set, running nothing. The fight's start ran the same way and got through by luck (other VGA state); D71's Resume Game check in the first menu was at the same risk. Fix: `run_cmd` runs `Z:\SYSTEM\CONFIG.COM` itself (no COMMAND.COM), its output put on handle 1 by SAVEHOOK.COM (create NUL or `SAVEHOOK.RSM`, 45h/46h, back after), for `speed` (D17) and D71's -get and -set; and `exec_room` runs it only when the largest free block, the reserve freed, holds `CONFIG_ROOM` A0h paragraphs, else the reserve is taken again and nothing runs (the speed stays as it was) instead of a hang. CONFIG.COM alone needs about 8Bh of the 100h. Tried in DOSBox-X with a test program (100h free, write mode 1): the old command line hung (140h was enough for it), the new one came back and set the cycles; with 80h free the new one still hung (its block, the environment taken, under 40h), with 60h the exec failed at once, hence the check | emulator-tested (`tests/test_resume.py`, DOSBox-X test program); not yet tried in play |

The Amiga's routine (`01A8C2`, called from the same five places of its script) plays one of 153, 154, 155, 153 (random; the dice rattle at three pitches, the last two after a short silence) on channel 3, which it reserves (`02A470`), so each shake cuts the one before. **The thunder:** the Amiga's henge script (the offering at Stonehenge, "The druids prepare for the ritual"; `03DD52`) plays sound 157 and then calls `01AAA8`, which plays it again 51 times in 128, on the same channel in the same frame (the music holds the other three), so one thunder is heard. The DOS script (DS:CFBD) has the same wait before it, no sound command, and calls `HengeThunderSnd` (1000:B5F2), an empty `ret`. **The wind is not there on the Amiga either:** nothing calls the loader of `Wn.a` (`02A5F4`) and nothing asks for program 62; program 79 is asked for only by a developer's test key (`011284`, `G` in a key menu at `01113C` that nothing enters; the Guardian fight's real start `0116EC` does not play it); nothing refers to 134. Those three stay out. Fix: `cmp word [8643h],2` at B508 becomes `int 63h` / `int 63h` / `ret`, and the henge script's call (DS:CFC1) points to the second int (B50A). SAVEHOOK.COM: `dice_snd` (with the Amiga effects one of 153, 154, 155, 153 by the BIOS clock's tick, so the game's random numbers are untouched, then the routine's own `call 5970`, whatever the music card; otherwise the compare) and `thunder_snd` (with the Amiga effects 157 through the same call; otherwise back to the `ret`). `amiga_fixed` plays 153-155 on channel 3 and reserves it, as D67's loops. The Amiga silences every channel and gives the reserved ones back at the end of a scene's fade-out (`018B46` -> `02A55A`): `music_stop` (D73: the tavern's, the henge's, the wizard's, Mythral's and the healer's end) now calls `amiga_stop` too, which closes D67's open point for these scenes. Nothing changes with the DOS effects. Without the disk images both have a substitute sound: the dice `sounds/dice.wav`, the thunder (the sample `he.a`, instrument 130) `sounds/thunder.wav` (15 Nov 2026): 1.53 s from 0.39 s of a CC0 recording ("sfx_thunder blast.wav" by bajko, Freesound 399656), high-passed at 250 Hz so that its energy lies mostly between 300 and 2000 Hz, the last 35 % faded out, at the Amiga sample's level (RMS 0.168 of full scale, the highest peaks rounded off under 0.97), 8 bits at 7830 Hz; the user chose it by ear from nine candidates (`make_recorded_sounds.py` `SHAPED`, `export_sound_programs.py` `SUBSTITUTES`). SAVEHOOK.COM grows by 82 bytes; the game's memory does not grow. | emulator-tested (`tests/test_amiga_scenes.py`; `tests/test_sound_programs.py` and `tools/test_review_fixes.py` for the thunder's substitute), autosave check; the substitute chosen by ear from a listening page; not yet heard in the game |

So a knight at base 5 with one Ring of Strength shows 6 (two: 7) and can do all a knight at base 5 can; the base cap itself does not rise with rings. The palette swatch `tools/map_palette.png` (ships with the tool; the user's request of 19 Nov 2026): the 32 colours as flat 56 x 56 squares, each exactly the expanded 12-bit colour the import matches, labelled with the index, the six free ones framed yellow; frames, text and background use no expanded 12-bit value; *Export template* writes `palette.png` with the project's own colours. | D136 | **Smooth animation: in-between pictures in fights** (unreleased; custom rules, `[Rules] smooth_animation = 1 \| 2 \| 3 \| 6` pictures a tick, 6, older ini files 6, speedrun mode 1, hardcore mode as set; `smooth_fight_cycles = auto \| 0 \| 100-65535`; `[Debug] draw_FPS`, `draw_TPS = no \| yes`; stage 1 of `docs/smooth-animation-plan-2026-11-19.md` and its decisions 1-11: positions only, no new poses, fights only) | A fight's logic keeps its tick of 2 BIOS ticks (6 interrupts of the game's own 54.6 Hz timer, 109.85 ms) and nothing of it runs twice; with the setting above 1 the tick's picture is held back and n - 1 in-between pictures are shown first. **Sites** (written by `rules.py` only with the setting above 1 or a read-out on; else MAIN.EXE has the game's own calls and the loop is the game's): the fight loop's flip `0363` (`call 5A4A`) -> `sm_hold`, which shows nothing (the picture stays in its page) and starts the lists at a fight's first pass; the tick's wait `036C` (`call 98CA`) -> `sm_tick`. **Display list**: `rules_draw`'s `draw_call` (the blits at `9AFB`, `9B07`) calls `smo_rec` after the colour state is set and before the blitter: 18 bytes a piece (bank, x, y, object record, frame, flags: shadow slot `95E6`, facing, also drawn into the background, `saber_piece`, `char_piece`, `gore_pass`; `recolour`, `rc_shift`, `sw_shift`) into two lists of 48 in the buffer block (`BUF_SM`, 1,728 bytes, this tick's and the one before; a list that runs over is not used). Nothing is noted while `spd_skip` is set (Speed's left-out frame never reaches `draw_call`), for a piece `rules_draw` does not draw, on the other screens, or before a fight's first `sm_hold` (`smo_arm`, cleared in `fight_handler`); the background's own draw (`9AFD`) only marks the piece. `rules_draw` itself runs once a piece, as before (`finish_hold`, `hold_refresh`). **What moves** (`smo_match`, once a tick): only whole objects. A piece continues when the piece of the tick before with the same place in its object's own order (the object record; a shadow is a piece of its object) has the same bank, frame, shadow flag and facing and is at most 48 pixels away (\|dx\| + \|dy\|), and the piece is not also drawn into the background. An object moves, each piece by its own step, only when every piece it has this tick continues; otherwise all of it is drawn in place, as the game draws it. So thrown daggers, the dragon's body parts and a figure pushed or thrown back in the same pose move in small steps, and a knight or monster whose pose changes (walking, blows) looks as in the original until in-between poses are built. *As first built* this was the plan's rule A (a piece matched by its picture anywhere in its object; a new piece took the median step of its object's continuing pieces). The user's playtest of 19 Nov 2026: walking was "floaty", and the dragon's breath broke up into chunks. Traced in `tests/test_smooth.py`'s runs: a walking knight's only continuing piece is its sword, whose step is 4 to 18 pixels a tick with up to 3 up or down, so the whole figure slid by the sword's step and bobbed, its planted foot sliding; and the breath's pieces change frames 24 and 25 among themselves, so each was paired with its neighbour's place, 41 or 42 pixels away, and drawn up to 35 pixels off. Picture k of n draws the tick's pieces in the tick's order, each (n - k) / n of its step back. A tick in which no piece has a step shows its own picture at once, as the game does. **Drawing** (`sm_tick`): each step is a game routine reached through `call_game` that returns to the site's int 63h again (`smo_phase`): the page restored from the background (`5A72`), each piece blitted (`5DD4`) with its colour state put back, after `79BC` when its frame is stored facing the other way; frames turned for a picture are turned back as the game's pass left them (collision reads frame pixels). No script, no control routine, no random number. **Pages**: the tick's picture stays in the game's back page `[80B9h]`; the in-between pictures go into the two other pages in turn (never the one on the screen; into the one shown before the last flip only after a vertical retrace has begun, port 3DAh bit 3, as the game waits at `5A30`), each shown by the CRTC's start address (index 0Ch, as `5A40`); at the end the tick's page is shown and `[80B7h]`, `[80B9h]` are set as `5A4A` sets them. Everything else a fight shows is in the background copy at AC00h (score, bars, mines, the idle-exit hint) and comes with the restore. **Clock**: `[0:046Ch]` x 3 + 3 - `[7C19h]` (the timer handler `5934` counts 3, 2, 1), read with interrupts off; no PIT write, no new interrupt handler. Picture k is due k x 6 / n sub-ticks into the tick, the tick's own at its end. **Governor**: a picture is drawn only when, by the last one's cost (whole sub-ticks, rounded up), it will be ready before the sub-tick after its time is over, else it is left out; one ready late is shown at once; if the pictures still make a tick a whole sub-tick late there is one picture a tick for 18 ticks (2 s). The logic never waits for a picture: with too few cycles the fight looks as before and keeps its pace. **Read-outs** (`smo_show`, the score's digits in the top left corner of every picture): pictures and ticks a second with one decimal, counted over 10 ticks from the BIOS ticks they took (a tick in which nothing moves counts as all its pictures). **Cycles** (D17's path: with the setting above 1 `fight_cycles` is replaced by `smooth_fight_cycles`, auto = 14,000, 20,000, 32,000 for 2, 3, 6): measured in DOSBox-X 2026.10.01 (70 Hz emulated screen, `research/smooth-animation-2026-11-19/fps_measure.py`, a measuring build that counts the pictures shown; knight 1 walking to and fro): the share of the in-between pictures shown in the ticks with movement. 6 a tick: a knight against the ratmen 74 % at 12,000 cycles, 96 % at 16,000; against the war beasts 100 % at 16,000; three knights against the trolls 56 % at 16,000, 80 % at 20,000, 89 % at 26,000, 94 % at 32,000, 95 % at 40,000. 3 a tick: the ratmen 58 % at 6,000, 88 % at 8,000, 100 % at 12,000; three knights and the trolls 59 % at 10,000, 89 % at 14,000, 95 % at 20,000. 2 a tick: the ratmen 18 % at 5,000, 93 % at 7,000, 100 % at 10,000; three knights and the trolls 23 % at 7,000, 64 % at 10,000, 93 % at 14,000 (the trolls' few per cent short are the ticks of their screen shake). Every run kept the game's pace, 9.14 ticks a second (8.66 with the trolls: the shake stops the logic for 15 retraces, as in the original), but for far too few cycles (2 a tick at 4,000 with three knights: 8.0); no tick was made late by the pictures at the auto values. With today's `fight_cycles` (2,600) and one picture a tick the same ratmen fight runs at 8.65: 162 of its 270 ticks were over when their picture was due. The plan's figures (7,000, 10,000, 14,000-24,000) were about half of what is needed: a picture costs about twice its instruction count in DOSBox-X (port accesses, the timer's music and sound work), and with the tick's picture held in its page every in-between picture after the first waits for a retrace of the 70 Hz screen before it can be drawn. Not measured: the dragon and four knights against a big monster (no practice start); `draw_FPS` shows them in play. (Measured with the first rule, under which a walking knight made every tick one with movement; a picture costs the same with the whole-object rule, which needs in-between pictures in far fewer ticks.) A fight waits for the BIOS tick, so the higher cycles do not make it faster; fights that overran `fight_cycles` now keep 9.1 ticks a second (decision 11; the trolls' screen shake still takes its 15 retraces). **Installer**: the buffer block, allocated next to the resident part, now reaches the top of the .COM's 64 KB where its stack was, and the XMS move of the terrain table into the block wrote over its own return address (the installer hung): the stack is moved to the end of the overlay window before the installer's tail runs. **Found**: `forcerate` (`[video]`) cannot be changed while the game runs: `CONFIG -set "video forcerate=60"` is stored but the screen stays at 70 Hz (350 retraces in 5 s before and after; `research/smooth-animation-2026-11-19/vrate/`); set in the conf file it works (299). The screen-rate choice (plan decision 5) is not built. Resident +1,692 bytes (+1,878 as first built; the whole-object rule is 186 smaller), the buffer block +1,728 (7,664). Tests: `tests/test_smooth.py` (MAIN's real fight loop from seven DOSBox-X saves through `research/dragon_fire/fightsim.py`, the clock driven by the suite: with 2, 3 and 6 pictures a tick the game's state after every tick is the same as with 1, bit for bit: all memory below A000h (MAIN's code, its data segment with the slots at DS:947E, every record, the random state, every bank), the background page, the page with the tick's picture, SAVEHOOK.COM's data and the buffer block, but for the feature's own variables and lists, the blitter's scratch (DS:815F-8175, its words in its code at `5F80`, `6355`, `6359`, `637B`-`638E`, the work buffer `[88F7h]`:0000-BFFF) and `call_game`'s two words; on a fast machine, a slow one and a clock that jumps at random; every in-between picture's blits against an independent reading of the rule from the game's own blits; in the game's own ticks a figure in a new pose whose sword goes on is drawn in place, a thrown dagger moves by its own step and the dragon's breath is drawn in place when its pieces change frames among themselves; the pages and flips; a frame turned on purpose; a list run over; the read-outs) | tested in Unicorn and in DOSBox-X (measured); played once (19 Nov 2026: daggers, swings and attacks fine, walking floaty, the dragon's breath in chunks, the pace a little faster) and again with the whole-object rule (the full pace is right, daggers smooth, walking jerky as in the original: its in-between poses are the plan's stage 3) |
