| D139 | **Special items in only some lairs: the lairs way** (unreleased; custom rules, `[Special_items] special_items = lairs` (or `on`), the new default, with `special_item_lairs = 6` (0-24); an older ini without `special_items` keeps `chance`; speedrun mode `off`; S2 of `docs/ini-rares-plan-2026-11-19.md`, work queue item 8, the user's decisions 6b, 7a, 8a, 9b of 19 Nov 2026) | Only N of the 24 lairs hold special items (sword and armour enchantments, special potions, rings, gems), each at least one; the others none. **`rules.py`:** `SPECIAL_MODES` lairs/on = 2 (`SPEC_LAIRS`), `read_ini` reads `special_item_lairs` (`[Special_items]` only, default 6), and three new table bytes after `G_DRAW_OUT`: `G_SPEC_MODE` (0 off, 1 chance, 2 lairs), `G_SPEC_LAIRS` (N) and `G_SPEC_W` (the five chances as read: sword, armour, potion, ring, gem). In the lairs way the five chance bytes are written as **100**, so the four roll sites (`holder_get`, `arm_get`, `pot_chest`, `kind_code`'s `P_chest`: each `cmp dl, chance / jae none` with dl 0-99) always give an item for a byte still 0, and a byte set to 1 stays none; none of the sites changed. **`spec_pick`** (resident, so also without XMS; called at the end of `knight_reset`'s knight-1 path right after its clear, only with `G_SPEC_MODE` = 2 and `[0704h]` not 2, i.e. not in practice): every byte of the five tables 1; the lairs where some kind is allowed listed (`sp_eff`: its on-byte, read at run time since the installer clears the potions', rings' and gems' without XMS; its weight above 0; not the sword where the Sword of Sharpness lies, `[items+4]` of the lair record `DS:0002 + 12h n`); min(N, their number) of them drawn by a partial shuffle of the list (`sp_list`, each set of lairs as likely, N draws); in each picked lair each allowed kind rolled at its weight (`sp_roll`, `game_random` % 100 < weight: byte 0); none came: one kind drawn by weight from those not yet drawn in this round (`sp_draw`, `sp_round` bits; no candidate: a new round when every kind allowed anywhere, `sp_all`, has come, else (the Sword of Sharpness lair with only the sword left) a draw from that lair's kinds that keeps the round); last the hoard's five rolls (`sp_roll` with holder 24; its Sword of Sharpness is tested at the look, as before). Checked over the code: nothing writes 0 to the five tables but `knight_reset`'s clear (every other write is 1 or 1 + an item), and the chance bytes are read only at the four roll sites. | D140 | **The lairs way spread over the four quarters, 8 lairs by default; a lair stays while a special item is left** (unreleased; custom rules, `[Special_items] special_item_lairs = 8` (was 6 in D139; also for an ini without the key); the user's two decisions of 19 Nov 2026 after D139, work queue item 8) | **The quarters.** The game's own four regions: `InitGameStart` puts one key in each block of six lairs (`1E2D`-`1E7E`: `NUM16` `1EEB` gives 0-5, the record pointer steps 6 x 12h a key), lairs 0-5 lower left, 6-11 upper right, 12-17 lower right, 18-23 upper left on the game's own map; taken by the lair's number (a map-tool project that moves lairs keeps their numbers). `spec_pick` now lists the lairs that can take a kind by quarter (`sp_list` six places a quarter, `sp_qn`), gives each quarter N / 4 (`sp_quota`) and the rest one more each in quarters drawn (`sp_take`, a partial shuffle) from those with a lair left above N / 4 (`sp_ql`; so only swords on with the Sword of Sharpness lair: N = 23 still gives 23), then draws each quarter's share from its list, rolls and draws as in D139, quarter by quarter, then the hoard. With 8: two in each. **Kept lairs.** New hook `0608` (`or ax, ax / jne 0616` in `05DD`, after the chest; ax = 0 when the lair's gold and 24 item bytes are all 0, and the game sets its `+0Ah`/`+0Ch` to FFFFh at `060C`) -> `lair_empty` (resident): the lair stays (on at `0616`) while one of the five kinds' bytes (`lair_slot`, 25 apart) holds an item (1 + it), or is 0 (not decided) with that kind's on-byte set (`sp_on`); in a practice the game's rule. Both ways. An undecided 0 means: in the lairs way an item the pick gave; in the chance way a potion, ring or gem whose roll waited for a looking human knight with a free place (enchantments are decided at the first look by anyone). Cost of that rule in the chance way: such a lair stays (showing nothing special yet) until a knight with a free place looks; then the roll is made and, if it gives none, the lair leaves at that visit. A kind that is off never keeps a lair. 060C is the only place that removes a lair; every other test (the map scan, the map draw, Greed, the aim, the computer knights' own choice) looks at x < 0, so a kept lair is an ordinary lair: entering it is a fight again (`InitLair` `1F2C`, no first-win XP: `[+8]`), then the chest, as the game does for a lair with items left. The smart computer knights (`bk_lair`, `A9B6`, D45) aim only at lairs with gold or game items, so never at one that holds only special items; computer knights never enter lairs anyway. The logic, the collision and the tick's own picture are the game's. **The frames** (`tools/walk_frames.py`, plain Python, run by Setup; `SAVEHOOK.INB` in the game folder, never distributed): for each of the four pose changes five body pictures at the sixths of the tick, for both knight looks (`KN1.OB`, `HE1.OB`) and both facings: 80 pictures by the layered recipe of the approved sample (`research/smooth-animation-2026-11-19/walk_poses.py`: the legs and torso piece composed, cut into the upper body, the two legs and their shadows, the upper body moved, each leg leaned about the hip row by row, the standing foot the origin, the old pose's layers up to the switch point and the new pose's after it; pixels only moved). Then the legs piece's list entry gets its step and `SMX_WALK` with the slots' number in `E_AUX`'s upper half, the torso's `SMX_SKIP`, the sword its own step, and `smo_moves` is set. Starting from standing, stopping, turning round, a blow, a hit, a Potion of Speed's doubled steps (a pose is skipped) and every other figure are not matched and are drawn as before. The walk-in at a fight's start is the same four poses and is matched too. **The picture** (`x143_body`, resident; picture k of n shows j = 6 x k / n sixths: 2 a tick the pose at 3/6, 3 a tick 2/6 and 4/6, 6 a tick all five): the legs entry's colour state is set as for any piece, the slot is fetched from XMS into the buffer block (`BUF_WALK`, 1,600 bytes: one body at a time, fetched just before it is drawn; a fetch that fails leaves the body out of that picture), the bank's data pointer is written, and the blitter draws its frame 0 in place of the legs piece; the torso piece is left out; the sword piece is the game's own, drawn (n - k) / n of its own step back as D136 draws a moving piece. Both facings are stored, so nothing is mirrored at run time (64 KB more of XMS against a pass through the game's `79BC` for every picture). **The place** (`wk_part`): the picture hangs on the standing foot, which goes in a straight line from where it stands in the old tick to where it stands in the new one; with the legs piece at xa the tick before and xb now, x = xa + (j x (xb - xa) + cx) / 6 - 128, where cx (one of the slot's two words) holds six times the constant part: the foot's place in the two poses and in the picture, the half for rounding and the bias; an exact half goes to the even pixel (the word's top bit), as the sample rounds. Nothing is added to the game's banks, the bank area or the EMS file cache. **Room**: resident +239 bytes with the data, the installer +99: 3,966 bytes free in the segment (4,304 before); the buffer block +1,600 bytes (9,264; in DOSBox-X SAVEHOOK at `CC4B`, the buffers at `DB29`, both in upper memory). **Measured in DOSBox-X** (`fps_measure.py`, 6 pictures a tick at the auto 32,000 cycles, knight 1 walking to and fro): a knight against the ratmen 100 % of the in-between pictures shown at 9.14 ticks a second; three knights against the trolls 93 % at 8.66 (the trolls' screen shake, as in D136: 94 % before). Without the attacks' files (`KN4.OB`, or a bank that is not the game's) `walk_frames.build` writes the walk's slots alone. **The installer**: one XMS block for the whole file (2,783 KB); a file of the walk's and the attacks' slots sets `atk_on`, one of the walk's slots only is the walk's (the attacks drawn in place); any other length or sum: no in-between poses (D143). **Which knights strike** (`atk_match`, once a tick after `walk_match`, only with `atk_on`, in the new overlay group 12 `OVL_ATK`, 849 bytes with its tables): an object's pieces (next to each other in the list) in both ticks are a pose (`x146_pose`: a knight's, type 6 or 8; the first piece of `KN1.OB` (`[8937h]`) or `HE1.OB` (`[894Dh]`), the others of that look's KN1/KN2 or HE1/HE2 or of `KN4.OB` (the look's bank table `8933h`/`8949h`); none a shadow, drawn into the background, a lightsaber's (D51) or a charred knight's (K42); the body pieces in the first piece's colour state and the KN4 pieces in the first KN4 piece's; the pose told by `atk_hash`, a 16-bit hash of the pieces' banks, frames and y from the first piece's and their count, which no other frame of the game's knight scripts has; its root from `ATK_POSES`' first piece, facing left with the frame's width from its bank), the two poses a change of `ATK_CHANGES`, the same look, the same facing, the same root, and no Potion of Speed works on the knight (`spd_on`: its extra steps leave frames out). Then the first piece's entry gets `SMX_ATK` (both `E_AUX` bits 04h and 08h) and the change's first slot for its facing and look in its `E_DX` word, the second piece's `E_DX` byte K and its `E_DY` byte the sword piece's offset, the knight's other pieces `SMX_SKIP`, and `smo_moves` is set. **The picture** (`x146_atk`, resident; `x136_piece` takes it by `jpe` after its `test al, 0Ch`): picture k of n shows j = 6 x k / n (`smo_g` while a picture is drawn: the walk's `x143_body` reads it there too now), so 2 a tick the picture at 3/6, 3 a tick 2/6 and 4/6, 6 a tick all five; slot E_DX + (j - 1) x K + `atk_s` is fetched into `BUF_WALK` (`wk_fetch`, the walk's fetch as a routine), its bank's segment written, and each frame is blitted with the colour state of its part's piece (`smo_state`, `x136_blit`'s as a routine) at the first piece's place + the record's; the state machine comes back to the same entry for each frame (`atk_f`, the next record) and slot (`atk_s`). A fetch that fails leaves the rest of the knight out of that picture. The tick's own picture is the game's. **Left drawn in place** (15.5, 15.6): the walk into a blow (no in-between from a walk pose), a knight under Speed, the struck knight's hit reactions, the deaths, the duck (D144), `10BA`, and the other attacks (the backward and upward thrusts, the dagger throw, the block and the dodge: the next step, which needs only recipes, poses and table rows). **Room**: resident +180 bytes with the data, the installer +25: 3,537 bytes free in the segment (3,742 before); group 12 849 bytes (the window stays 1,268); the buffer block unchanged; XMS 2.8 MB (of about 15). **Measured in DOSBox-X**: a knight striking the ratmen at 6 a tick: 100 % of the in-between pictures shown, 9.14 ticks a second; three knights against the trolls at 6, 3 and 2 a tick: 93, 93 and 91 %, 8.70 ticks a second (the walk's shares and pace, D143); the whole file read (`atk_on` 1); the autosave check gave a FIGHT save state with the attacks on (plan 16.2). Tests: `tests/test_attack_frames.py` (the poses, changes and recipe are the sample's, with the fixes' way round; the 420 pictures of both looks equal the approved fixes' sample's pixel for pixel, and the as-built maker's the recipe sample's; the parts and strips read back as banks give each picture at its place, both facings; the file's layout, sum and `ATK_K`; without `KN4.OB` the walk's slots alone; `atk_hash` against the game's knight scripts; Setup's time), `tests/test_walk_frames.py` (the installer: the full file sets `atk_on`, the walk's slots alone do not, a changed, short or long file of either kind gives no poses), `tests/test_smooth.py` (as D136 and D143, and with both knights of `teams4.sav` striking each other, blocking and dodging: the game's state bit for bit as with 1 picture a tick at 2, 3 and 6 with a fast, a slow and a jumping clock; every in-between picture against an independent reading: a knight in a change of `ATK_CHANGES` (both looks, both facings) is the maker's strips with its pieces' colour states at the maker's places, read back from the bank the blitter is given; every other pose change in place; with the walk's slots only every blow in place; knight 1 under a Potion of Speed in place while knight 2's blows have their pictures) | tested in Unicorn and measured in DOSBox-X; not played: the look in play is the user's to judge (`USERTESTS.md` D146) |

D15 sets `[0978h]` for each object before its update, so from the handler it was the dragon's own knight, but in the animation pass it is what the last object's update left: the first knight standing (blue). During each far fire the dragon drifted to blue's depth (as far as D68's reach lets it), the fire crossed green only in its 7th and 8th frames, and D68's `dragon_burn` drops every far-fire hit before the 14th (`FAR_SPARE`). With one knight `[0978h]` is always that knight, so the original game cannot show it. **The fix:** the site 3C00 (`a1 78 09` -> int 63h + nop) -> `dragon_aim` (resident, 47 bytes; 3,531 -> 3,480 free): in co-op (`coop` not 0), not `monster_target = first` (`TARGET_FIRST`) and not HOTSTUFF (`dragon_ctl`), the walk takes the dragon's own knight, D15's `last_target` at the dragon's place (20), while that knight stands; else `[0978h]` as the game. In the handler that is the value `[0978h]` already holds (D90's `finish_pick` knight included: the picked knight is then down), so only the walk run from the scripts changes; the fire then hurts that knight from its 14th frame as D68 intends (one that runs gets away). **Checked for the same leak:** the game's script-called routines (op 98h in every script in DS) that read `[0978h]` are `CountTheDead` (0213, the knights' scripts: meant to be the first knight standing), `TrackKnight` (this fix), and the Guardian's `KnightOFF`/`KnightON` (5254, 5262: K40's `demon_off`/`demon_on` already give them the Guardian's knight); `FlipKnight` (3D1F/3D36) is called only from `RatmanHit` (352D, the ratman's own update, D15's value; the ratmen fight is not changed) and 3816 is the Balok's release (`ControlBalokRelease`, reached from its update; K28's `balok_free` at 3812). So only the dragon's needed it. **Tests:** `tests/test_dragon_aim.py` (the handler alone: co-op, no co-op, `first`, HOTSTUFF, the knight down at HP 0 and -1, none picked, si and bx kept; the real fight loop of the user's slot 3 (a copy) with this build's SAVEHOOK.COM: before, nobody hurt in 150 frames and the walk in the fire aimed at blue; now green burnt at frame 4 of the run and blue at frame 30; `monster_target = first`, one knight and HOTSTUFF frame by frame as before). | emulator-tested (Unicorn, the full suites, the repo regression, the autosave check); not played (`USERTESTS.md` D150) |

The axe trogg keeps the game's flinch and D125's tie. **The code (resident):** hooks `2F28` `trogg_struck` (`c6454a00`: a hammer trogg in move 10h struck by a player's knight's riposte keeps its wait and sets `rip_keep`; else the game's `mov byte [di+4Ah],0`), `gun_monster` (2F46, after 2F43's damage `2D73`, so D35's enchantments and D109's Vampirism count once): with `rip_keep` the damage is taken there and, alive, the control ends with si = -1 (`2D72`, a `ret`: the engine at 9905 keeps the blow's script running) or, when its blow hit in the same frame, goes to `TroggHit`; a kill clears the wait and takes the game's struck script. `55E7` `troll_tie` (`837d0c00`, `ControlTroll`'s own-hit test, which came first and lost a riposte landing in the same frame): with a riposte on it the struck path `TrollStruck` (56CC) runs first for the damage; `56DC` `troll_flinch` (`c7063a78945c`, after the damage and 57B4's hit mark) leaves out the flinch script 5C94 as above (`rip_go`). `td_tie`'s monsters are `TIE_MONSTERS` = the axe trogg only. **2. | D152 | **A tapped back key turns the knight** (unreleased; custom rules, `input_chord` above 0 (2, the default), no new setting; user's playtest of 21 Nov 2026, item 3: "a short tap backwards does not turn the knight"; study `docs/study-2026-11-22-s4-walk-taps.md` (fix 1), B5 in `docs/next-steps-2026-11-22.md`) | **Why:** D24 keeps a release back until the key has been down 3 BIOS ticks, so a press of 0-2 ticks is seen by exactly one of the fight's key reads (one every 2 ticks); D24's chord wait (`input_chord`, `rules_input` at 3F16) makes the first read with a back direction and no fire stand (`chord_on` 1) and turns only at a later read that still has the back key, which a tap never gives, so the wait ended with no turn (the same with `smooth_animation` 1 and 6; the user's INPUT.LOG: 16 % of the left/right presses were that short). **The fix:** in `rules_input` the read with no back direction (`ich_back`) goes to `ich_gone` (after the routine's end): with a wait running (`chord_on` 1), no forward key in the read (fire was already cleared before), a keyboard knight (`+9` 1, 2 or 7: the joysticks 0, 5, 6 and the computer as before) and the read at most `CHORD_STALE` (9) ticks after the wait began, the back bit is put back into the read and the wait goes on as for a held key: the knight stands until `input_chord` ticks have passed, then turns (D25's turn, no step), with any up or down of that read kept (D141's `turn_note` notes a down held from before as none: a plain turn). A read with fire or the forward key clears the wait as before, so the chords (back + down + fire the block, back + up + fire the dagger), D142's backward thrust and the input buffer's turn are unchanged. A read more than 9 ticks after the wait began (a hit reaction, a pause or the fight's end came between) drops the wait with the read as it was. The back key let go and pressed again within the wait no longer starts the wait again: the turn comes `input_chord` ticks after the first press. **Room:** 57 bytes resident (3,110 -> 3,053 free). **Tests:** `tests/test_input.py` (D152 cases: the tap turns at the wait's end at either parity, a tap seen by two reads as before, facing left, forward or fire or the block chord in the wait, down kept, player 2 and the third key set, the joysticks unchanged, the stale wait dropped, `input_chord` 0, down held from before then fire: the lunge the new way; D24's "let go: the wait starts again" case changed to the new rule); the study's replay through the real input path (`research/walk-taps-2026-11-22/tapsim.py back` with the dev tools, `out_back_d152.txt`): the 0.5-tick tap that did nothing now turns the knight 110 ms after its read. | emulator-tested (Unicorn, the full suites, the repo regression, the autosave check); not played (`USERTESTS.md` D152) |
| D153 | **Fights and turns start quickly again; no automatic save at a practice fight** (unreleased; no new setting; the user's report of 22 Nov 2026, "every fight starts really slowly", and of 24 Nov 2026, the same before every knight's turn; study `docs/study-2026-11-22-s6-fight-start.md`, the user's choices A, B and C in its section 9, B7 in `docs/next-steps-2026-11-22.md`) | **Why:** the automatic save before a fight (D6) and at a human turn's start (D7) is DOSBox-X's save state of the whole machine, deflated at level 9 (`[dosbox] compresssaveparts = true`, DOSBox-X's default) while the emulation stands still; since D146-D148 the 5.3 MB `SAVEHOOK.INB` lies in XMS, and its short and middle-length zero runs (the strips' transparent pixels) make level 9 walk its whole hash chain at every run's end: the file alone took about 2.0 s to deflate on this machine (0.05 s with D143's walk only), the whole save 2.8-4.3 s against 0.6-1.0 s before (measured with Python's zlib on the user's own saves). **B, the fix:** every slot of `SAVEHOOK.INB` but the header is packed in place (`walk_frames.pack_slot`, run by Setup for the walk's and the attacks' slots): the slot's 1,600 bytes as a stream of a nonzero byte (itself) or a zero and a count (1-255 zeros); the slot's first `WK_PACK` (1,300) bytes are a word (where the stream starts), zeros and the stream ending at `WK_PACK`, the rest of the slot zero, each slot at its old place (the file keeps its 5,281,600 bytes). `wk_fetch` moves those 1,300 bytes (`wk_move`'s length) to the end of `BUF_WALK`'s room (`WK_SRC`, the room `WK_SLOT` + `WK_MARGIN` = 39 bytes; `BUF_ILOG` moved up past it) and unpacks them forward into `BUF_WALK` until it is full (`wk_tok`: `lods gs:`, `stosb` or `rep stosb`), keeping cx, si, di, ds, es; ZF = 1 only when the XMS move failed. The maker checks for every slot that the stream fits and that the unpacking never overtakes the bytes still to be read (`WK_PACK` and `WK_MARGIN` are the longest packed slot and the largest margin, measured on the game's files); a slot that does not fit leaves the attacks out (or, a walk slot, the file not made: the poses drawn in place). `WK_MAGIC` `INB2`, `WK_SUM` = 0x4B58 + `WK_PACK` + `WK_MARGIN` + `BUF_WALK`: an unpacked file of D143-D152 is refused by the installer and the poses are drawn in place until Setup makes the file again. Level-9 deflate of the file (Python's zlib, this machine): 1.7-1.8 s -> 0.22-0.23 s, its deflated size about the same (965 KB against 956 KB). **C:** `save_state` (shared by fights and turns) goes to `save_skip` after the save-mode test when MAIN's `[0704h]` is 2 (the menu's practice: every practice duel, co-op, two-player, monster and practice lair fight; the map's turns never come with it): no FIGHTn rename, no wait for DOSBox-X's once-a-second check, no save; D17's speed, D120's, D127's, D132's and D136's resets, `checkpoint` (the token of `autosave_only` and `none`), `amiga_stop` and `gods_quiet` as before. **A:** `moonstone-dosbox-x.conf` writes out `compresssaveparts = true` with a comment: `false` stores every save unpacked, quicker, but about 41 MB a file (the nine automatic slots about 370 MB instead of about 20 MB). **Room:** 59 bytes resident (3,053 -> 2,994 free; the decoder 46, the practice test 13), the buffer block 39 bytes more. **Tests:** `tests/test_walk_frames.py` (every slot packed as `pack_slot` packs it, within `WK_PACK` and `WK_MARGIN`, unpacked as `wk_fetch` does it it is the old slot; the deflate time; an unpacked file of D143-D152 refused), `tests/test_attack_frames.py` (every attack slot packed), `tests/test_smooth.py` (the in-between pictures drawn from the packed file through the real `wk_fetch`, the game's state as with 1 picture), `tests/test_duck.py`, `tests/test_saves.py` (D153: no save at a practice fight of one knight, a co-op of three, two players, the speed still set and the resets done, the checkpoint token still made; a fight of the game saved as before; the conf's line), the repo regression (`test_packed_slots`, the conf). DOSBox-X: the autosave check (`testw.conf`, whose keys start a menu practice fight, so with the test copy's practice test patched off; the third run made `save/100.sav`, 2,080,820 bytes): the packed file whole in XMS, `wk_h` 2, `atk_on` 1; that save's parts deflate at level 9 in 0.94 s against 2.75 s for the check's D148-era save (Python's zlib). Not measured: DOSBox-X's own save time and the wait in wall-clock seconds (the machine is loaded; study section 7); the in-between pictures and a practice fight with no save not seen in DOSBox-X. | emulator-tested (Unicorn, the suites by filter, the repo regression, the autosave check); not played (`USERTESTS.md` D153) |
| D154 | **A tapped walk moves a little** (unreleased; custom rules, new setting `[Rules] walk_short_first_step = on`, default on, also for an older ini; user's playtest of 21 Nov 2026, item 1: a small tap should give a small move, "23 or 25 is too much, 3 or 4 pixels is OK"; study `docs/study-2026-11-22-s4-walk-taps.md` (fix 2, option B), the user's choice B of 24 Nov 2026, B5 in `docs/next-steps-2026-11-22.md`) | **Why:** a tap of left or right is one read and one walk step, and the game's steps are uneven by the walk phase (`+0Ah`, DS:77FE: 25, 3, 23, 4 pixels for the new phase 0-3), so every second tap jumped 23-25 pixels in one tick (the same with `smooth_animation` 1 and 6). **The fix:** a hook at 3F61 (`add byte [di+0Ah],1`, the phase moved on for a step, after 3F5D saved it at [783Ch]) runs `rules_walk_short`: when the knight did not walk at its last read, the read has left or right (diagonals too) and the phase is odd (the step would be a big one), the phase is moved on by one more, so the first step from standing is the short one (3 or 4 pixels) and a held walk takes its big step second; then the replaced add (3F65 masks with 3). The game's own steps, poses and collision are unchanged, and 4022 still puts the saved phase back when the step is refused (a turn, D25's `walk_block`, a figure in the way). `walk_st` (4 bytes, per knight): the phase after the last step | 04h; `walk_seen`, called first in `rules_input` (3F16, every read of ControlKnight): 08h when the last read took a step whose phase still stands (a refused step counts as standing), else 0. A turn (D25) moves the phase on too and keeps it, so `rules_walk_block`'s turning branch clears the knight's 04h (found with D155: a held back key turned the knight and its first step was a big one). Up or down alone, a walk that goes on, Speed (D104) and every knight that reads input through ControlKnight (computer knights too) as described; off: the hook is not written (the game's add). **Room:** 103 bytes resident (2,994 -> 2,891 free), the turn's part 17 more (with D155). **Tests:** `tests/test_input.py` (D154: the hook bytes on and off; four taps from each phase all 3-4 pixels; off as the game; right and diagonal taps; a held walk from standing 4, 25, 3, 23, 4; up alone unchanged; a refused step then forward short again; a turn is no step (walk_st 05h -> 01h); up then left at the next read unchanged; a computer knight); all 84 suites, the repo regression; the autosave check (`testw.conf`, the copy's practice test patched off as for D153: `save/96.sav` made). | emulator-tested (Unicorn, the full suites, the repo regression, the autosave check); not played (`USERTESTS.md` D154) |
| D155 | **A back tap turns the knight also while the forward key is still held** (unreleased; custom rules, no new setting; the user's playtest of 24 Nov 2026: "a short tap the other way does not always turn the knight, so D152 is not quite complete") | **Why:** found by replaying taps through the real input path (`research/walk-taps-2026-11-22/tapsim.py`'s `Run` with the dev tools and the new hooks written into the save's MAIN): taps from standing all turned (56 lengths and timings), but a back tap pressed before the forward key was let go (walking, then back) did nothing: GetInputDevice (0A8C, ControlKnight's only read) drops both left and right when both are in a read (0AA1 `and ax,0FCh`), and a tap of 0-2 ticks is seen by exactly that one read (D24), so D24's chord wait never began and D152 had nothing to finish. **The fix:** a hook at 0AA1 (`rules_lr_newest`, reached only with both held): for a keyboard knight (`+9` 2, 1, 7) the one of the two held keys of its player's set (`key_dirs`, MAIN's key table) pressed later (`key_tick`, BIOS ticks, compared as a difference) is kept, the older dropped, so the back key starts the chord wait and D152 turns the knight when it is let go; pressed in the same tick, a joystick or a computer knight: both dropped as in the game; up and down together stay the game's. A back key pressed and held while forward is held now turns the knight and walks it back (the game stood still); let go, the forward key alone turns it again. Back + fire together stays the backward thrust (D142). **Room:** 127 bytes resident (2,891 -> 2,747 free with D154's turn part). **Tests:** `tests/test_input.py` (D155: the newer of left and right kept either way, the same tick dropped, up and fire kept, player 2 and the third key set, a computer and a joystick dropped, one held not reached, up + down as the game); the replay (`research/walk-taps-2026-11-22/taps_d155.py ctx`, `grid`): back taps 1 tick and 0.3 tick before forward's release now turn, the 56 taps from standing, taps after fire, two quick taps, taps while walking up or down all turn. | emulator-tested (Unicorn, the full suites, the repo regression, the autosave check); not played (`USERTESTS.md` D155) |
| D156 | **A back tap turns the knight even when the forward key is let go late** (unreleased; custom rules, no new setting; the user's playtest of 24 Nov 2026 after D155: "when the green knight walks right (D) and then taps left (A), nothing happens") | **Why:** replayed for player 2 (W A S D) with `research/walk-taps-2026-11-22/taps_d155.py p2ctx` (knight 1 put on `+9` 1; the replay's joystick B read 82DA now off as 834D was, else player 2 got phantom joystick input): a tap of A over a held D turned the knight only when D was let go within about 3 ticks of the tap; let go later, the next read had D (the forward key) and D152's `ich_gone` dropped the wait: no turn. A tap seen by two reads with D still held turned the knight left and at once back right (`chord_on` still 2, D now the back key). **The fix:** `lr_newest` notes the older key it drops (`lr_drop`); `rules_input` (after `knight_no`) notes this read's horizontal keys held, as read or dropped (`raw_h`), and a key in the knight's `fwd_mask` that is still held is taken out of the read (not counted), the mask cleared when it is let go; the chord wait notes the forward key held as it began (`chord_fwd`, = `lr_drop`), and `ich_gone` with the forward key in the read goes on to the turn when that key is the one held from the wait's start (dropped from the read), and clears the wait as before when it was pressed in the wait; `face` (D25's turn) puts the old facing's key into `fwd_mask` when `raw_h` has it. So a key held from before a turn the other way counts for nothing until it is let go and pressed again: the knight stays facing the tap's way. Changes D155's "let go of back, forward still held: it turns again": the knight now stands until forward is pressed again. Taps from standing, quick double taps, the chords and the joysticks as before. **Not reproduced:** the user's "walking right, then left + up: up first for a while, then left + up" (and the same with down, and walking left then right + up): the replay with W pressed with A, half a tick before or after and with D let go before or after shows one read standing (the chord wait), then left + up; waits for an INPUT.LOG (written at a fight's end, 0ABE) or a keyboard check (W A S D on some keyboards drop a third key held). **Room:** 122 bytes resident (2,747 -> 2,625 free). **Tests:** `tests/test_input.py` (D156: the turn with the forward key still held from the wait's start, no turn with one pressed in the wait, a masked key not counted, let go, counted again, masked while dropped by `lr_newest`, face's mask set only with the old key held); the replay: `p2ctx` (taps of 0.6 and 2.5 ticks, D let go 1.5 ticks before to 9 ticks after: all turn left and stay), `ctx`, `grid` (56 taps). The suites that move a practice save's SAVEHOOK state into this build (`research/fight_saves/newhook.py`'s `transplant`, used by `test_riposte_d151.py`; `test_dragon_aim.py`'s own) assumed one shift for each half of `rules_data`: with D154-D156's variables inside it, a reference's coincidental match outnumbered the real ones and a part of the timed defence's state came from the wrong place. Now the check labels must be among their references' deltas, each span takes its own references' delta (else the one before), and the variables newer than the saves are zeroed (`NEWER_STATE`). | emulator-tested (Unicorn, the full suites, the repo regression, the autosave check); not played (`USERTESTS.md` D156) |
| D157 | **A dropped key press still turns the knight; a diagonal turn goes diagonally at once** (unreleased; custom rules, no new setting; the user's playtest of 24 Nov 2026 after D156, with `INPUT.LOG`: "the green knight walking right does not turn left", "the blue knight going down-left, then up-right goes up for a while first") | **Why (from the user's INPUT.LOG, the fight of 24 Nov 2026 19:32):** every time A was pressed while D was held, the game got only A's release (ticks 445, 491, 581 after the fight's start: a key up with no key down), never its press: the keyboard or the host dropped the press, so no code could see it; every A pressed with D up turned the knight (413-415, 459-461, 527-529). The blue knight (fight 231): left + down, then right + up: one read standing (D24's chord wait), then the turn read moved up only (D25's turn takes no step left or right, `walk_block`), then up + right. **The fix:** (1) `key_handler`'s release: a key let go that MAIN's key table holds as released with no release pending (its press never came), a direction key (`key_dir`) in a fight (`fight_begun`): taken as a tap: pressed now (`key_tick`, `kh_dir_new`), its release held back as D24's (`key_pending` 1, `key_settle`), logged as kind 8 (`ILOG_KEY_GHOST`, `tools/input_log.py`); a press `cheat_key` keeps from the game marks its key (`key_pending` 2) so that key's release stays a plain release; not a fire key, not outside a fight. The turn then comes when the key is let go (the user's log: about 0.3 s after D). (2) `rules_walk_block`'s turning branch: a turn with a step in depth (`[783Fh]` not 0) goes on to the step checks with its step left or right (D154: the short one), so a diagonal the other way moves diagonally on its turn read; a turn without up or down stays D25's (no step). **Room:** 110 bytes resident (2,625 -> 2,515 free). **Tests:** `tests/test_input.py` (D157: a release with no press in a fight pressed now with its release held back, the arrows too, not outside a fight, not a fire key, not a cheat code's key; a turn with a depth step keeps its x step, without none); the replay `research/walk-taps-2026-11-22/taps_d155.py` (`p2` with only A's release: the knight turns left; `show` left + down then right + up: the turn read moves up + right; `ctx`, `grid`, `p2ctx` as before; the replay now sets `fight_begun`, set at 036F past its frame's end). | emulator-tested (Unicorn, the full suites, the repo regression, the autosave check); not played (`USERTESTS.md` D157) |
| D76 | **Gore with four values** (0.9.1; user request, 13 Nov 2026) | The first menu's Gore switch goes round Off, Less, On, More (`gore_level` in SAVEHOOK.COM, 0-3), remembered between starts in `C:\SAVEHOOK.GOR` (one byte; custom rules `gore`, default On, until the file is there). **MAIN.EXE:** OptionKeys' toggle for item 1 (`1000:1359`, `xor word [0700h],1`, reached by fire, left and right) becomes int 63h + nop x3: `rules_gore_step` steps one value back for left (the game's bx bit 2 without fire), else one on, round the values allowed (`gore_max`: K18 all four; K16 or `death_animations = off` Off, Less; K12 Off only), and sets `[0700h]` (1 for Off, else 0), which the game and D22 read. DoOptions' value text (`139F`, `mov word [050Eh],0581h`, then `13A5`-`13B1` picked "On" by `[0700h]`) becomes int 63h + `jmp 13B2` + nop x2: `rules_gore_text` points the value record (`DS:050E`, x 214) at "Off" (`0581`), "On" (`057E`) or "Less"/"More", written to `DS:FE80` (KEY_TEXT, used only on the map and the item screen). **Less** = K16: `deaths_gore` gives ZF = 0 below On, so no D20 deaths, no beheading (`pick_death`), no crush (troll, Balok), no monster special deaths (`mnf_set`, `sbf_set`). **More** = ABACABB: `abacabb_eff` sets `cheat_eff` = `cheat_flags`, with ABACABB for More, which `spawn_blood` (three times the blood) and `cheat_finish` (a death animation at once) test; the code itself is unchanged and toggles only its own flag. **Less draws the deaths without blood** (playtest 13 Nov: the Balok's bite and the boar's kill still bled; Less only stopped the deaths that `deaths_gore` already turned away). Six more of the game's `cmp word [0700h],0` become int 63h + nop x3. Where a death is chosen (`gore_death`, ZF from `deaths_gore`, so the game takes its own gore-off way with Less and K16): `2E88` (a deadly sweep: the beheading), `2F89` and `434D` (the spear trogg impales a knight down: death 12, now the plain death as with gore off), `4446` (the boar's kill of a knight with no HP left, scripts `3270`/`30F2`: now its toss `4487`, death 4). Where a death's blood is drawn (`gore_slot`, di = the animation slot): `9A73` (pieces flagged 80h, the blood pieces, are skipped) and `9CFD` (the scripts' gore branch, opcode 8Ch, jumps to its gore-off target); D22's `set_gore_pass` (the pixels of colour 15) does the same. They follow `less_death`: with Gore Less (also K16 and `death_animations = off`, which hold it there) a slot is drawn as with gore off when its object (+16h) is a knight, Black Knight or monster (+35h 0, 2, 4, 6, 8, 0Ch, 0Eh, 10h, 12h, 18h, 20h; not the hit effects 14h, daggers, fire) with HP 0 or less (death 1's slump and red patch, every monster's own death), the Balok holding (`[7794h]` 21h) a knight down (`balok_victim`: deaths 8 and 9, the bite and the squeeze), or the boar (`boar_obj`, K42) with the knight it struck (`boar_victim`) down (death 4). The blood of the hits stays: the game's blood bursts are objects of type 14h, and a knight still standing keeps its hit reaction's blood. Deaths 2, 3 and the D20 deaths were already the plain death; the dragon's eating (6) has no blood of its own (the red is the knight, drawn as D22 leaves it); the mudmen (10, 11), the Balok's shake (7), the fireball (5) and the Guardian's zap (K42, charred) have none; ratmen, troggs, trolls and Black Knights kill by death 1. **Remembered:** `gore_load` (from `menu_open`, D71's site 1279, each time the first menu opens) reads SAVEHOOK.GOR (0-3; no file or another byte: the value stays); `gore_step` writes it (`gore_save`, int 21h create/write) when the value changed, so a press that the age rating keeps from changing anything (K12) leaves the file, and a choice the ini no longer allows is shown lowered but kept for later. **`death_animations = off`** no longer turns the blood off in a fight (`pick_death` set `[0700h]` = 1 at the first death): it is Gore Less, `gore_max` at most Less. **Age rating:** `gore_lock` (fight frame, map loop, first menu, and both new sites) lowers `gore_level` to `gore_max` and keeps `[0700h]` = 1 for Off. Works in the fixed game without custom rules (table defaults: K18, deaths extended): all four values; the cheat codes stay off there, More does not need them. Saves and loaded DOSBox-X states do not change the file | tested in Unicorn (`tests/test_gore_menu.py`: the sites, the steps both ways in the fixed game and with custom rules, the texts, K16, K12, `death_animations = off`, the `gore` setting, the file read and written (fake int 21h), `deaths_gore` and the troll's crush for Less, `cheat_eff` for More, the menu again after a fight; the four death sites for Off, Less, On, More and K16; the draw's, the script's and the pixels' way for a knight down and standing, a troll down, a hit effect, the Balok biting, holding a knight alive or none, the boar's toss of a knight down or alive and another boar, K16). Playtest 13 Nov: all four values at a fresh start; only On/Off after a fight back in the menu, which came from a DOSBox-X state saved before D76 (a state carries its own MAIN.EXE and SAVEHOOK.COM: every save of 13 Nov 20:36-21:00 has the original `xor` at 1359); in the current build the fight returns to `00A8`, which calls DoOptions (`1241`) again through the D76 sites |

The DOS port has one voice and asks for one of them. With the Amiga effects on, SAVEHOOK.COM's sound handler (`rules_sound_play`, `1000:5979`) now goes on after a note (`sp_more`) and plays what the Amiga plays with it, on the channels after it. No change to MAIN.EXE. **From a table** (`amiga_more`, `AMIGA_MORE` in `patches.py`: id, the id after it): the mudmen's voice (Amiga `03CBC8`: 87+148, 88+149, 89+150, 151+152; DOS asks for 87, 88, 89 or 152 since K35), the Guardian's death (`03D24E`: 64+65, 66+67 or 68+69) and the Guardian's yell (`03D224`: 74, 75, 76, 77). **The Guardian's death** is one of three screams on the Amiga, picked at random; the DOS routine (`1000:581C`) always asks for 64, so the handler picks 64, 66 or 68 by the low bits of the BIOS clock's tick (1: 66, 2: 68, else 64), which leaves the game's own random numbers alone. **The yell** differs from the Amiga on purpose: there its four sounds reserve all four channels and nothing gives them back before the fight ends (only the dragon's scripts and the end of a fight clear the mask at `02A502`), so no other sound could play after a yell; here they take the four channels in turn and reserve none. **Once a request also:** a troll's grunt (136-142; `1000:5801`) is followed by a second one from the game's table (`DS:7DB6`, K35), picked by the clock, as the Amiga's `03CE22` plays two random picks; and the dragon's landing (45, asked for by `1000:3760`: the handler looks at the sound routine's return address, 3763h) is followed by 46, as at the Amiga's `016D52`. A 45 from a script stays alone. A sound that follows needs a free channel like any other: with one free channel it cuts the first, with none nothing plays. Nothing of this happens with the Amiga effects off: with one voice the second sound would only cut the first. The AdLib tune file holds its own player, and with velocity-0 notes the intro froze where the tune ends (80 s), with a tone left on; since 0.9.0 its notes stay as they are and the player's note-on loses the key-on bit instead (`or ah,20h` at 96Ch of the file becomes `or ah,0`). ## Kept as in the original

(The ratmen disease is not kept: see K17.)

The random number bias, plate armour giving no movement bonus, the lack of wandering monsters, the lack of hit
invulnerability, the computer knights' extra experience, and winning past the 150 gold limit in the dice game.

## Known bugs not fixed yet

| ID | Bug |
|---|---|
| K8 | The end summary names *constitution* twice and leaves out endurance (reported). |
| - | The axe and hammer troggs strike the air at the edge of their reach (the original game; found with D124/D125, 19 Nov 2026). `TroggAttack` (1000:2E70) picks by the distance bx: up to 100 the swing (2EE0: against a knight not blocking always the swing; against a block 30 % the chop), 101-120 the chop (2F0B), farther a step. In MAIN's fight loop the axe trogg's swing reaches only to 98, so at 99-100 it swings at the air for as long as the knight stands there; the hammer trogg's swing misses at 100 and its chop at 101-104. Not the troll's `<=`/`<` mismatch of K43 (the troggs' tests agree with each other): a reach gap. A fix would pick the attack that reaches there. |
| - | A weapon piece drawn mirrored samples the struck figure at the wrong column (the original game; found with D131, 19 Nov 2026). In the hit test's per-point loop (`A2B3`) a point's bounds use the mirrored x (`[C2C2h]` - x, `A2B6`-`A2C6`), but the pixel read uses the raw x (`A303`, `mov cl,[bx-2]`), so for every figure facing the mirrored way the point is tested at the column mirrored about the weapon piece. It changes where blows land for every fighter facing that way, so it is kept; D131 covers the troll's club both ways. |
